\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|^{2}\leq 2\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|\cdot\|\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\alpha}^{t},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{t}))\|\leq 2C_{0}(1+\sqrt{\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)}+\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|)\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|\leq 4.1C_{0}(1+\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t))+3C_{0}\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|^{2}

(where we again relax a constant 4 to 4.1).
Recalling the equation for
\Phi_{\alpha}
in (
23
),

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\Phi_{\alpha}(t)=4.1C_{0}(1+\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t))+3C_{0}\Phi_{\alpha}(t),

since
C_{0}>0
and
\|\tilde{\alpha}^{0}\|^{2}=\Phi_{\alpha}(0)
,
this shows
\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{\alpha}(t)
.
∎

Next we equip the spaces
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
with metrics. Fixing a
large constant
\lambda>0
, define

\displaystyle d(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\|\alpha_{1}^{t}-\alpha_{2}^{t}\|

(47)

\displaystyle d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{(w_{1}^{\ast},\{w_{1}^{t}\})\sim C_{\theta}^{1},(w_{2}^{\ast},\{w_{2}^{t}\})\sim C_{\theta}^{2}}\Big[\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(w_{1}^{*}-w_{2}^{*})^{2}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(w_{1}^{t}-w_{2}^{t})^{2}}\Big]

\displaystyle d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})

\displaystyle=\inf_{\{u_{1}^{t}\}\sim C_{\eta}^{1},\{u_{2}^{t}\}\sim C_{\eta}^{2}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(u_{1}^{t}-u_{2}^{t})^{2}}

\displaystyle d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\big|R_{\theta}^{1}(t,s)-R_{\theta}^{2}(t,s)\big|

\displaystyle d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2})

\displaystyle=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\big|R_{\eta}^{1}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(t,s)\big|.

In the definitions of
d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})
and

d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})
above,
the infima are taken over all couplings of mean-zero
Gaussian processes with covariances

(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})
and
(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})
.
Writing
X^{i}=(R_{\eta}^{i},C_{\eta}^{i},\alpha_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and

Y^{i}=(R_{\theta}^{i},C_{\theta}^{i},\tilde{\alpha}_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}

for
i=1,2
, let

\displaystyle d(X^{1},X^{2})

\displaystyle=d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2})+d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})+d(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}),

(48)

\displaystyle d(Y^{1},Y^{2})

\displaystyle=d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})+d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})+d(\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2}).

(49)

Lemma 3.7

(Modulus of
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}
)
.

Let
X^{i}=(R_{\eta}^{i},C_{\eta}^{i},\alpha_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and

Y^{i}=\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}(X^{i})=(R_{\theta}^{i},C_{\theta}^{i},\tilde{\alpha}_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
for
i=1,2
. Then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a constant

\lambda=\lambda(\varepsilon)>0
sufficiently large defining the metrics
(
47
) such that

\displaystyle d(Y^{1},Y^{2})\leq\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2}).

Proof.

We write
C,C^{\prime}>0
for constants that may depend on
T
, but not on
\lambda
,
and changing from instance to instance.

Bound of
d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})
.

Let
\{u_{1}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\{u_{2}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be an optimal coupling
in the definition of
d(C^{1}_{\eta},C^{2}_{\eta})
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(u_{1}^{t}-u_{2}^{t})^{2}]}=d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2}).

(50)

Let
\{\theta_{i}^{t}\}
be the solution to
(
12
) driven by
\{u_{i}^{t},\alpha_{i}^{t},R_{\eta}^{i}\}

for
i=1,2
, with a common Brownian motion
\{b^{t}\}
and initialization

\theta^{0}
, i.e.

\displaystyle\theta_{i}^{t}=\theta^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big({-}\delta\beta(\theta^{s}_{i}-\theta^{\ast})+s(\theta^{s}_{i},\alpha^{s}_{i})+\int_{0}^{s}R^{i}_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta_{i}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+u^{s}_{i}\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}b^{t}.

(51)

By definition, we have
\mathbb{E}[\theta_{1}^{t}\theta_{1}^{s}]=\mathbb{E}[\theta_{2}^{t}\theta_{2}^{s}]=C_{\theta}(t,s)
. Moreover,

\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t})^{2}\leq 5[(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)]

where we set

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}\delta\beta|\theta^{s}_{1}-\theta^{s}_{2}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|s(\theta^{s}_{1},\alpha^{s}_{1})-s(\theta^{s}_{2},\alpha^{s}_{2})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|\theta^{s^{\prime}}_{1}-\theta^{s^{\prime}}_{2}|\Big(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}|R^{1}_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{*}|\Big(\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}|R^{1}_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})-R_{\eta}^{2}(s,s^{\prime})|\mathrm{d}s\Big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle(V)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{t}|u^{s}_{1}-u^{s}_{2}|\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2}.

Term
(I)
satisfies

\displaystyle(I)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{s}_{1}-\theta^{s}_{2})^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=C\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{s}_{1}-\theta^{s}_{2})^{2}\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}.

To bound
(II)
, applying the Lipschitz properties of
s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.2
and a similar argument,

\displaystyle(II)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{s}-\theta_{2}^{s})^{2}+\|\alpha_{1}^{s}-\alpha_{2}^{s}\|^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\Big(\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t})^{2}+\|\alpha_{1}^{t}-\alpha_{2}^{t}\|^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{t}-\theta_{2}^{t})^{2}+d(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})^{2}\Big).

For
(III)
, using the condition
|R^{1}_{\eta}(t,s)|\leq\Phi_{R_{\eta}}(t-s)\leq C
, we have

\displaystyle(III)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{s}-\theta_{2}^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}.

For
(IV)
, using
\mathbb{E}(\theta^{s}_{2}-\theta^{*})^{2}\leq 2C_{\theta}(s,s)+2\tau_{*}^{2}\leq 2\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(s)+2\tau_{*}^{2}\leq C
, we have

\displaystyle(IV)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}\big(R_{\eta}^{1}(s,s^{\prime})-R_{\eta}^{2}(s,s^{\prime})\big)^{2}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{s^{\prime}}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\cdot\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-2\lambda t}\big(R_{\eta}^{1}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(t,s)\big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2})^{2}.

Lastly for
(V)
, using (
50
), we have

\displaystyle(V)\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}(u^{s}_{1}-u^{s}_{2})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\cdot d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})^{2}.

Combining these bounds, for a constant
C>0
independent of
\lambda
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\Big(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}+d(X^{1},X^{2})^{2}\Big).

Thus for any
\varepsilon>0
, choosing
\lambda=\lambda(\varepsilon)
large enough and
rearranging gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}\leq\varepsilon^{2}d(X^{1},X^{2})^{2}.

Finally, let
(w^{*},\{w_{1}^{t}\},\{w_{2}^{t}\})

be a centered Gaussian process with second moments matching

(\theta^{*},\{\theta_{1}^{t}\},\{\theta_{2}^{t}\})
. Then

(w^{*},\{w_{1}^{t}\})
and
(w^{*},\{w_{2}^{t}\})
realizes a coupling defining the metric

d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})
in (
47
), so

d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2})^{2}}=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}_{1}-\theta^{t}_{2})^{2}}\leq\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2}).

(52)

Bound of
d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})
.
Defining
the processes
\frac{\partial\theta_{i}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}
for
i=1,2
from the
above coupling of
\{\theta_{1}^{t}\}
and
\{\theta_{2}^{t}\}
,
by definition we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta_{i}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}=1-\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\delta\beta-\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{i}^{s^{\prime}},\alpha^{s^{\prime}}_{i})\Big)\frac{\partial\theta_{i}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}R^{i}_{\eta}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\frac{\partial\theta_{i}^{s^{\prime\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\Big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

Then

\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\leq 4[(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)]

where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}},\alpha^{s^{\prime}}_{1})-\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}},\alpha^{s^{\prime}}_{2})\Big|\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\Big]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big(\delta|\beta|+|\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}},\alpha^{s^{\prime}}_{2})|\Big)\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\Big]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|R^{1}_{\eta}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})-R^{2}_{\eta}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\Big|\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\Big]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=\int_{s}^{t}\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\Big[|R^{2}_{\eta}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})|\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\Big]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

For
(I)
, note that (
46
) implies

|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}|\leq C
for a constant
C>0

with probability 1. Then,
using the Lipschitz continuity of
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle(I)\leq C\int_{s}^{t}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big|\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\big|+\|\alpha_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\alpha_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\|\Big)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s}^{t}e^{\lambda s^{\prime}}\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda s^{\prime}}\Big(\mathbb{E}\big|\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\big|+\|\alpha_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\alpha_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\sup_{s^{\prime}\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda s^{\prime}}\Big(\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\big(\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\big)^{2}}+\|\alpha_{1}^{s^{\prime}}-\alpha_{2}^{s^{\prime}}\|\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\Big(\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2})+d(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})\Big),

the last step using (
52
) already shown. For
(II)
, applying
the boundedness of
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.2
, we have

\displaystyle(II)\leq C\int_{s}^{t}e^{\lambda s^{\prime}}e^{-\lambda s^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\Big]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\cdot\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|.

For
(III)
, applying again (
46
) to bound

|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{s^{\prime}}}{\partial u^{s}}|\leq C
,

(III)\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\cdot d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2}).

For
(IV)
, applying
|R^{2}_{\eta}(t,s)|\leq\Phi_{R_{\eta}}(t-s)\leq C
,

\displaystyle(IV)\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|.

Combining these bounds,

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|+d(X^{1},X^{2})\Big),

so rearranging and choosing
\lambda=\lambda(\varepsilon)
large enough gives

d(R^{1}_{\theta},R_{\theta}^{2})\leq\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}\Big|\frac{\partial\theta_{1}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}-\frac{\partial\theta_{2}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}\Big|\leq\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2}).

(53)

Bound of
d(\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2})
.
By definition,

\tilde{\alpha}_{i}^{t}=\alpha^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\alpha}_{i}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\theta_{i}^{s}))\mathrm{d}s

for
i=1,2
. Letting
\{\theta_{1}^{t}\}
and
\{\theta_{2}^{t}\}
be coupled as above and applying Assumption
2.3
,

\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{t}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{t}\|\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{s}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{s}\|+W_{2}(\mathsf{P}(\theta_{1}^{s}),\mathsf{P}(\theta_{2}^{s}))\Big)\mathrm{d}s\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{s}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{s}\|+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{s}-\theta_{2}^{s})^{2}}\Big)\mathrm{d}s.

Then

\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{t}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{t}\|\leq C\int_{0}^{t}e^{\lambda s}\mathrm{d}s\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda s}\Big(\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{s}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{s}\|+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\theta_{1}^{s}-\theta_{2}^{s})^{2}}\Big)\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\Big(d(\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2})+\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2})\Big).

Choosing
\lambda=\lambda(\varepsilon)
large enough and rearranging shows

d(\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\|\tilde{\alpha}_{1}^{t}-\tilde{\alpha}_{2}^{t}\|\leq\varepsilon\cdot d(X^{1},X^{2}).

(54)

The lemma follows from (
52
), (
53
),
and (
54
).
∎

Lemma 3.8

(Modulus of
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}
)
.

Let
Y^{i}=(R_{\theta}^{i},C_{\theta}^{i},\tilde{\alpha}_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
and

X^{i}=\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{i})=(C_{\eta}^{i},R_{\eta}^{i},\alpha_{i})\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}

for
i=1,2
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for any sufficiently
large
\lambda>0
defining the metrics (
47
),

\displaystyle d(X^{1},X^{2})\leq C\cdot d(Y^{1},Y^{2}).

Proof.

The proof is similar to that of Lemma
3.7
so we will
omit some details. Again let
C,C^{\prime},C^{\prime\prime}>0
denote constants depending on
T
but
not on
\lambda
.

Bound of
d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})
.

Let
(w_{1}^{*},\{w^{t}_{1}\})
and
(w_{2}^{*},\{w^{t}_{2}\})
be an optimal coupling for
which

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(w^{\ast}_{1}-w^{\ast}_{2})^{2}]}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(w^{t}_{1}-w^{t}_{2})^{2}]}=d(C^{1}_{\theta},C^{2}_{\theta}).

For
i=1,2
, let

\displaystyle\eta^{t}_{i}

\displaystyle=-\beta\int_{0}^{t}R^{i}_{\theta}(t,s)\big(\eta_{i}^{s}+w^{\ast}_{i}-\varepsilon\big)\mathrm{d}s-w^{t}_{i}

be the corresponding coupled solutions to (
14
).
We write
\xi^{t}_{i}=\eta^{t}_{i}+w^{\ast}_{i}-\varepsilon
, so that

\displaystyle\xi^{t}_{i}=-\beta\int_{0}^{t}R^{i}_{\theta}(t,s)\xi_{i}^{s}\mathrm{d}s-w^{t}_{i}+w^{\ast}_{i}-\varepsilon

and
C_{\eta}^{i}(t,s)=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[\xi_{i}^{t}\xi_{i}^{s}]
. Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}_{1}-\xi^{t}_{2})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big[\int_{0}^{t}(R^{1}_{\theta}(t,s)-R^{2}_{\theta}(t,s))^{2}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{1}^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}R^{2}_{\theta}(t,s)^{2}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{1}^{s}-\xi^{s}_{2})^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}(w_{1}^{t}-w_{1}^{*}-w_{2}^{t}+w_{2}^{*})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}\Big[\int_{0}^{t}e^{2\lambda s}\cdot e^{-2\lambda s}\Big((R^{1}_{\theta}(t,s)-R^{2}_{\theta}(t,s))^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\xi_{1}^{s}-\xi^{s}_{2})^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\mathbb{E}(w_{1}^{t}-w_{1}^{*}-w_{2}^{t}+w_{2}^{*})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime\prime}}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\Big(\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}(\xi_{1}^{s}-\xi^{s}_{2})^{2}+d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})^{2}\Big)+C^{\prime\prime}e^{2\lambda t}d(C_{\theta}^{1},C_{\theta}^{2})^{2}.

Choosing
\lambda>2C^{\prime\prime}
and rearranging yields, for a constant
C>0
,

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}_{1}-\xi^{t}_{2})^{2}\leq C\cdot d(Y^{1},Y^{2})^{2}.

Then letting
(\{u^{t}_{1}\},\{u^{t}_{2}\})
be a centered Gaussian process with
second moments
\mathbb{E}[u^{t}_{1}u^{s}_{2}]=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[\xi^{t}_{1}\xi^{s}_{2}]
,
this realizes a coupling defining
d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})
, so

\displaystyle d(C_{\eta}^{1},C_{\eta}^{2})\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[(u^{t}_{1}-u^{t}_{2})^{2}]}=\sqrt{\delta\beta^{2}}\cdot\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\xi^{t}_{1}-\xi^{t}_{2})^{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\cdot d(Y^{1},Y^{2}).

Bound of
d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2})
.

Defining the (deterministic) process
\frac{\partial\eta_{i}^{t}}{\partial w^{s}}

driven by
R_{\theta}^{i}
for
i=1,2
, we have

\displaystyle R_{\eta}^{i}(t,s)=-\beta\int_{s}^{t}R_{\theta}^{i}(t,s^{\prime})R_{\eta}^{i}(s^{\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\delta\beta^{2}R_{\theta}^{i}(t,s),

hence

\displaystyle|R^{1}_{\eta}(t,s)-R^{2}_{\eta}(t,s)|

\displaystyle\leq|\beta|\int_{s}^{t}|R_{\theta}^{1}(t,s^{\prime})-R_{\theta}^{2}(t,s^{\prime})||R_{\eta}^{1}(s^{\prime},s)|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+|\beta|\int_{s}^{t}|R_{\theta}^{2}(t,s^{\prime})||R_{\eta}^{1}(s^{\prime},s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(s^{\prime},s)|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\delta\beta^{2}|R^{1}_{\theta}(t,s)-R^{2}_{\theta}(t,s)|

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{s}^{t}e^{\lambda s^{\prime}}e^{-\lambda s^{\prime}}|R_{\eta}^{1}(s^{\prime},s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(s^{\prime},s)|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+Ce^{\lambda t}d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C^{\prime}}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}|R_{\eta}^{1}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(t,s)|\Big)+Ce^{\lambda t}d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2}).

Choosing
\lambda>2C^{\prime}
and rearranging yields

\displaystyle d(R_{\eta}^{1},R_{\eta}^{2})=\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}|R_{\eta}^{1}(t,s)-R_{\eta}^{2}(t,s)|\leq C\cdot d(R_{\theta}^{1},R_{\theta}^{2})\leq C\cdot d(Y^{1},Y^{2}).

We note that
\alpha_{i}=\tilde{\alpha}_{i}
for
i=1,2
by definition, so also

d(\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2})=d(\tilde{\alpha}_{1},\tilde{\alpha}_{2})\leq d(Y^{1},Y^{2})
.
Combining these bounds shows the lemma.
∎

Proof of Theorem
2.4
(b).

Combining Lemmas
3.7
and

3.8
, for sufficiently large
\lambda>0
,
the composition map
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}
is a contraction on

\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}}
with respect to the metric
d(X^{1},X^{2})

and similarly
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}
is a contraction on

\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
. We note that for any sequence

\{C_{\theta}^{k}\}
of correlation functions in
\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}
, as
k\to\infty
,

d(C_{\theta}^{k},C_{\theta})\to 0\text{ implies }\sup_{s,t\in[0,T]}|C_{\theta}^{k}(s,t)-C_{\theta}(s,t)|\to 0

by definition of the metric and Cauchy-Schwarz, while

\sup_{s,t\in[0,T]}|C_{\theta}^{k}(s,t)-C_{\theta}(s,t)|\to 0\text{ implies }d(C_{\theta}^{k},C_{\theta})\to 0

by e.g. the construction of a coupling in
[
52
, Lemma D.1]
.
The same holds for
C_{\eta}
, so
each metric in (
47
) induces a topology equivalent to
that of uniform convergence over
continuous functions on the appropriate space

[0,T]
,
\{s,t:0\leq s\leq t\leq T\}
, or

\{*\}\cup\{s,t:0\leq s\leq t\leq T\}
.
Furthermore, each condition
defining
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}},\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
is closed with respect
to this topology. Thus
d(X_{\eta}^{1},X_{\eta}^{2})
and

d(Y_{\theta}^{1},Y_{\theta}^{2})
are complete metrics on

\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}},\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
,
so the Banach fixed-point theorem guarantees
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}
have
unique fixed points
X=(R_{\eta},C_{\eta},\alpha)\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}}
and
Y=(R_{\theta},C_{\theta},\alpha)\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
, for which also
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}(X)=Y
.
These fixed points remain unique in
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
, because
Lemmas
3.5
and
3.6
imply
that the images of
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta},\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}
on

\mathcal{S}_{\eta},\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
are contained in

\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}},\mathcal{S}_{\theta}^{\text{cont}}
.
Then the tuple
(\alpha,C_{\theta},C_{\eta},R_{\theta},R_{\eta})\in\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}

is the unique fixed point in
\mathcal{S}
solving the dynamical fixed point
equations (
17
–
18
).
∎

4
The dynamical mean-field approximation

In this section we prove Theorem
2.5
. We assume throughout Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
, and
2.3
.
The proof consists of three steps:

•

(
Step 1
) We prove in Section
4.1

a discrete DMFT limit for a discretized version of the dynamics.

•

(
Step 2
) We show in Section
4.2
that, as the discretization step size goes to
zero, the discrete DMFT equations converge in an appropriate
sense to (
12
–
18
).

•

(
Step 3
) We show in Section
4.3

that, as the discretization step size goes to
zero, the discretized dynamics converges in an appropriate sense to
(
4
–
5
).

This argument follows closely the approach of
[
42
]
,
although we will use in Steps 2 and 3 a different and somewhat simpler
piecewise-constant embedding of the discretized DMFT process and discretized
Langevin dynamics into continuous time.

4.1
Step 1: DMFT approximation of discrete
dynamics

Fix a step size
\gamma>0
. We first define a discretized version of the process
(
4
–
5
), which we denote by

\{\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
and
\{\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}\}

for
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}=\{0,1,2,\ldots\}
:

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t+1}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}+\gamma\Big({-}\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{y})+s(\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma})\Big)+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t})

(55)

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta^{t}_{\gamma,j}}\Big)

(56)

with initialization

(\bm{\theta}^{0}_{\gamma},\widehat{\alpha}^{0}_{\gamma})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
, where

\{\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
is a discrete
Gaussian process with
\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{0}=0
and independent increments

\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\gamma\mathbf{I})
.
Here and throughout the sequel, we write as shorthand

s(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})=(s(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha}))_{j=1}^{d}
. We set

\bm{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t},\qquad\bm{\eta}^{*}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{*}.

We correspondingly define a discretized version of the DMFT system
(
12
–
18
): Given
discrete-time correlation and response matrices

\{C_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r\leq s\leq t}
,
\{R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t}
and a deterministic process
\{\alpha_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s\leq t}
up to time
t
,
define (in the probability space of

(\theta^{*},\theta^{0})\sim\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{0})
)

\displaystyle\theta^{t+1}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\theta^{t}_{\gamma}+\gamma\Big({-}\delta\beta(\theta^{t}_{\gamma}-\theta^{\ast})+s(\theta^{t}_{\gamma},\alpha^{t}_{\gamma})+\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)(\theta^{s}_{\gamma}-\theta^{\ast})+u^{t}_{\gamma}\Big)+\sqrt{2}(b_{\gamma}^{t+1}-b_{\gamma}^{t})

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\text{ with }\theta_{0}^{\gamma}=\theta^{0},

(57)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\theta^{t+1}_{\gamma}}{\partial u^{s}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\gamma&\text{ for }s=t,\\
\frac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{s}}+\gamma\Big[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\theta^{t}_{\gamma},\alpha^{t}_{\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{s}}+\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(t,r)\frac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{r}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{s}}\Big]&\text{ for }s<t.\end{cases}

(58)

Here,
\{u_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
and

\{b_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
are mean-zero Gaussian vectors independent
of each other and of
(\theta^{*},\theta^{0})
, where

\{u_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
has covariance

\mathbb{E}[u_{\gamma}^{s}u_{\gamma}^{r}]=C_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r),

(59)

and
\{b_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t}
has independent increments

b_{\gamma}^{s+1}-b_{\gamma}^{s}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\gamma)
with
b_{\gamma}^{0}=0
. We note that

\frac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{t+1}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{s}}
is the usual partial
derivative of
\theta_{\gamma}^{t+1}
in
u_{\gamma}^{s}
,
whose form (
58
) is derived from
(
57
) via the chain rule. These processes then define

\{C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r\leq s\leq t+1}
,

\{C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,*)\}_{s\leq t+1}
,

\{R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t+1}
,
and
\{\alpha_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s\leq t+1}
up to time
t+1
via

\begin{gathered}C^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s,r)=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{s}_{\gamma}\theta^{r}_{\gamma}],\quad C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,\ast)=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{s}_{\gamma}\theta^{\ast}],\quad C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(*,*)=\mathbb{E}[(\theta^{*})^{2}],\\
R^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s,r)=\mathbb{E}\Big[{\frac{\partial\theta^{s}_{\gamma}}{\partial u^{r}_{\gamma}}}\Big],\quad\alpha_{\gamma}^{t+1}=\alpha_{\gamma}^{t}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}(\alpha_{\gamma}^{t},\mathsf{P}(\theta_{\gamma}^{t}))\end{gathered}

(60)

where
\mathsf{P}(\theta_{\gamma}^{t})
is the law of
\theta_{\gamma}^{t}
.

Conversely, given
\{C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r\leq s\leq t}
,

\{C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,*)\}_{s\leq t}
, and

\{R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t}
up to time
t
, define (in the
probability space of
\varepsilon\sim\mathsf{P}(\varepsilon)
)

\displaystyle\eta^{t}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}R^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)(\eta^{s}_{\gamma}+w^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)-w^{t}_{\gamma},

(61)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{s}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\beta\Big[{-}\sum_{r=s+1}^{t-1}R^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,r)\frac{\partial\eta^{r}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{s}_{\gamma}}+R^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)\Big]\quad\text{ for }s<t.

(62)

Here,
(w_{\gamma}^{*},\{w_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{0\leq s\leq t})
is a mean-zero Gaussian
vector with covariance

\mathbb{E}[w^{s}_{\gamma}w^{r}_{\gamma}]=C^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s,r),\qquad\mathbb{E}[w^{s}_{\gamma}w^{\ast}_{\gamma}]=C^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s,\ast),\qquad\mathbb{E}[(w_{\gamma}^{*})^{2}]=C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(*,*),

(63)

and again
\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{s}_{\gamma}}
is the usual
partial derivative computed from the chain rule. These define

\{C_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r\leq s\leq t}
,

\{R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t}
up to time
t
via

C^{\gamma}_{\eta}(s,r)=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\eta^{s}_{\gamma}+w^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)(\eta_{\gamma}^{r}+w^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)],\quad R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(s,r)=\delta\beta\,\Big(\frac{\partial\eta^{s}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{r}_{\gamma}}\Big),

(64)

where we note that
\frac{\partial\eta^{s}_{\gamma}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{r}}
is
deterministic.
These definitions should be understood in the iterative sense

\begin{gathered}\{\theta_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s\leq t},\{u^{s}_{\gamma}\}_{s<t},\{\tfrac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{s}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{r}}\}_{r<s\leq t}\Rightarrow\{C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r),C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,*)\}_{r\leq s\leq t},\{R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t},\{\alpha^{s}\}_{s\leq t}\Rightarrow\\
w_{\gamma}^{*},\{\eta_{\gamma}^{s},w_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s\leq t},\{\tfrac{\partial\eta_{\gamma}^{s}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{r}}\}_{r<s\leq t}\Rightarrow\{C_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r\leq s\leq t},\{R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,r)\}_{r<s\leq t}\Rightarrow\\
\{\theta_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s\leq t+1},\{u_{\gamma}^{s}\}_{s<t+1},\{\tfrac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{s}}{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{r}}\}_{r<s\leq t+1}\Rightarrow\ldots\end{gathered}

(65)

with initialization
\theta_{\gamma}^{0}=\theta^{0}
.

The goal of this section is to show the following discrete analogue of Theorem

2.5
.

Lemma 4.1
.

For any fixed integer
T\geq 0
, almost surely as
n,d\rightarrow\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta^{\ast}_{j},\theta^{0}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\gamma,j}\big)}

\displaystyle\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\gamma}\big)

(66)

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\big(\eta^{*}_{i},\varepsilon_{i},\eta^{0}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\eta^{0}_{\gamma,i}\big)}

\displaystyle\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big({-}w_{\gamma}^{\ast},\varepsilon,\eta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\eta^{T}_{\gamma}\big)

(67)

\displaystyle(\widehat{\alpha}^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\widehat{\alpha}^{T}_{\gamma})

\displaystyle\rightarrow(\alpha^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\alpha^{T}_{\gamma}).

(68)

For convenience of the proof,
we define also an auxiliary response function

\displaystyle R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(t,\ast)=\delta\beta\,\Big(\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{\ast}_{\gamma}}\Big)\quad\text{ where
}\quad\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{\ast}}

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)\Big(\frac{\partial\eta^{s}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{\ast}_{\gamma}}+1\Big),

(69)

initialized from
\frac{\partial\eta^{0}_{\gamma}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{\ast}}=0
.
Here
\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{*}}

is the usual partial derivative of
\eta^{t}_{\gamma}
with respect to

w_{\gamma}^{\ast}
, which is also deterministic.
We have the following basic fact relating the response functions (
62
) and (
69
).

Lemma 4.2
.

For any
t\geq 1
, we have

\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{\ast}_{\gamma}}=-\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{s}_{\gamma}}
,
and consequently
R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,\ast)=-\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
.

Proof.

Let us shorthand
r_{\eta}(t,s)=\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{s}_{\gamma}}
for
s<t
and
r_{\eta}(t,\ast)=\frac{\partial\eta^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{\ast}_{\gamma}}
.
We prove
r_{\eta}(t,*)={-}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}r_{\eta}(t,s)
by
induction, with the base case
t=1
verified by the initial conditions

r_{\eta}(1,\ast)=-\gamma\beta
and
r_{\eta}(1,0)=\gamma\beta
.
Suppose the claim holds for some
t
, then

\displaystyle r_{\eta}(t+1,\ast)

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)(r_{\eta}(s,\ast)+1)

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)\Big({-}\sum_{r=0}^{s-1}r_{\eta}(s,r)+1\Big)

\displaystyle=\beta\Big[\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\sum_{s=r+1}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)r_{\eta}(s,r)-\sum_{r=0}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,r)\Big]

\displaystyle=\beta\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}\Big(\sum_{s=r+1}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)r_{\eta}(s,r)-R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,r)\Big)-\beta R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,t)

\displaystyle={-}\sum_{r=0}^{t-1}r_{\eta}(t+1,r)-r_{\eta}(t+1,t)={-}\sum_{r=0}^{t}r_{\eta}(t+1,r),

as desired.
∎

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

Step 1: Convergence of auxiliary dynamics.

Consider the following non-adaptive auxiliary dynamics

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t+1}=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\gamma\bigg(\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}-\bm{\varepsilon})-s(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma},\alpha^{t}_{\gamma})\bigg)+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t}).

(70)

This differs from
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}\}
in that
we replace
\{\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}\}
by the deterministic process

\{\alpha^{t}_{\gamma}\}
of the discrete DMFT system.
Let
\tilde{\bm{\eta}}^{t}_{\gamma}=\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}
. We
will first show

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta^{\ast}_{j},\tilde{\theta}^{0}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\tilde{\theta}^{T}_{\gamma,j}\big)}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\gamma}\big),\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\big(\eta^{*}_{i},\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{\eta}^{0}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\tilde{\eta}^{T}_{\gamma,i}\big)}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big({-}w_{\gamma}^{\ast},\varepsilon,\eta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\eta^{T}_{\gamma}\big).

(71)

The proof is based on a reduction to an AMP algorithm: Let

\bm{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be as in the above dynamics, define

\mathbf{V}=(\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{b}^{1}-\mathbf{b}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{b}^{T}-\mathbf{b}^{T-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(T+2)}

and let

\varepsilon\sim\mathsf{P}(\varepsilon),\qquad V=(\theta^{*},\theta^{0},\rho^{1},\ldots,\rho^{T})\sim\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{0})\otimes\mathcal{N}(0,\gamma I_{T}).

Assumption
2.1
ensures that
|\varepsilon|
and
\|v\|_{2}
have finite moment
generating functions in a neighborhood of 0, and
for each fixed
p\geq 1
, almost surely as
n,d\to\infty
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\varepsilon_{i}}\overset{W_{p}}{\to}\varepsilon,\quad\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{V_{j}}\overset{W_{p}}{\to}V

(72)

where
V_{j}
is the
j^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{V}
.
Fixing some
k\geq 1
, consider the AMP iterations

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{i}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}g_{i}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{i};\mathbf{V})-\sum_{j=0}^{i-1}f_{j}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{j};\bm{\varepsilon})\zeta_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times k},

(73)

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{i+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}^{\top}f_{i}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{i};\bm{\varepsilon})-\sum_{j=0}^{i}g_{j}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{j};\mathbf{V})\xi_{ij}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times k},

initialized at
\mathbf{W}^{0}=\mathbf{X}g_{0}(\mathbf{V})
, where the nonlinearities

\displaystyle f_{i}

\displaystyle=\big(f_{i,1},\ldots,f_{i,k}\big):\mathbb{R}^{k(i+1)}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{k},\qquad g_{i}=\big(g_{i,1},\ldots,g_{i,k}\big):\mathbb{R}^{ki}\times\mathbb{R}^{T+2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{k}

are Lipschitz-continuous and
applied row-wise, the Onsager coefficients are recursively defined as

\displaystyle\xi_{ij}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\delta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{d}_{W^{j}}f_{i}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{i};\varepsilon)\Big]\bigg)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},\quad 0\leq j\leq i,

\displaystyle\zeta_{ij}

\displaystyle=\bigg(\mathbb{E}\Big[\mathrm{d}_{U^{j+1}}g_{i}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V)\Big]\bigg)^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},\quad 0\leq j\leq i-1,

and
\{W^{j}\}_{j\geq 0}
and
\{U^{j}\}_{j\geq 1}
are mean-zero
Gaussian processes in
\mathbb{R}^{k}
independent of
\varepsilon,V
with covariance structure

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[W^{i}W^{j\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[g_{i}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V)g_{j}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)^{\top}\Big]\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},\quad i,j\geq 0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[U^{i+1}U^{j+1\top}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\delta f_{i}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{i};\varepsilon)f_{j}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j};\varepsilon)^{\top}\Big]\in\mathbb{R}^{k\times k},\quad i,j\geq 0.

(74)

This is a standard form of an AMP algorithm, see e.g.
[
45
,
60
]
. The iterations
for
(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{T-1})\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times kT}
and

(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{T})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times kT}
admit a mapping to the form of

[
60
, Eqs. (2.14) and (D.1–D.2)]
with
kT
vector
iterates. Then by the AMP state evolution (c.f.
[
60
, Theorem 2.21 and Remark 2.2]
), under the conditions
of Assumption
2.1
, almost surely as
n,d\rightarrow\infty
,

\begin{gathered}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{U^{1}_{j},\ldots,U^{m}_{j},V_{j}}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{m},V),\\
\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{W^{0}_{i},\ldots,W^{m}_{i},\varepsilon_{i}}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{m},\varepsilon).\end{gathered}

(75)

We will now use the above state evolution to prove the desired conclusion
(
71
). In the AMP algorithm
(
73
), let
k=2
. We show the existence of Lipschitz nonlinearities

g_{i}=(g_{i,1},g_{i,2}):\mathbb{R}^{2i}\times\mathbb{R}^{T+2}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
and

f_{i}=(f_{i,1},f_{i,2}):\mathbb{R}^{2(i+1)}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{2}
such that

\displaystyle(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma},\bm{\theta}^{\ast})

\displaystyle=g_{j}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{j};\mathbf{V}),

(76)

\displaystyle\Big({-}(\beta/\delta)(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}-\bm{\varepsilon}),0\Big)

\displaystyle=f_{j}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{j};\bm{\varepsilon}).

(77)

The base case is
g_{0}(\mathbf{V})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\bm{\theta}^{*})
and

f_{0}(\mathbf{W}^{0};\bm{\varepsilon})=({-}(\beta/\delta)(\mathbf{W}^{0}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{0}_{2}-\bm{\varepsilon}),0)

where
\mathbf{W}^{0}=(\mathbf{W}_{1}^{0},\mathbf{W}_{2}^{0})=(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{0},\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast})
.
Supposing inductively that
(
76
–
77
) hold for some Lipschitz
functions
g_{0},f_{0},\ldots,g_{j},f_{j}
, we note that this implies

(\xi_{j\ell})_{12}=(\xi_{j\ell})_{22}=0
for all
\ell\leq j
.
Then writing
\mathbf{U}^{j}=(\mathbf{U}^{j}_{1},\mathbf{U}^{j}_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times 2}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{j+1}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}^{\top}f_{j,1}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{j};\bm{\varepsilon})-\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\Big(g_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{\ell};\mathbf{V})(\xi_{j\ell})_{11}+g_{\ell,2}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{\ell};\mathbf{V})(\xi_{j\ell})_{21}\Big)

\displaystyle={-}\frac{\beta}{\delta}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}-\bm{\varepsilon})-\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}g_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{\ell};\mathbf{V})(\xi_{j\ell})_{11}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(\xi_{j\ell})_{21}\cdot\bm{\theta}^{\ast}.

So

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j+1}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma}-\gamma\bigg(\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}-\bm{\varepsilon})-s(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma},\alpha^{j}_{\gamma})\bigg)+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}^{j+1}-\mathbf{b}^{j})

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j}_{\gamma}+\gamma\delta\Big(\mathbf{U}^{j+1}_{1}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}g_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{\ell};\mathbf{V})(\xi_{j\ell})_{11}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(\xi_{j\ell})_{21}\cdot\bm{\theta}^{\ast}\Big)+\gamma s(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma},\alpha^{j}_{\gamma})+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}^{j+1}-\mathbf{b}^{j}),

and to satisfy (
76
) we may define
g_{j+1}(\cdot)
as

g_{j+1,2}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j+1};V)=\theta^{\ast}
and

\displaystyle g_{j+1,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j+1};V)

\displaystyle=g_{j,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)+\gamma\delta\Big(U^{j+1}_{1}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}g_{\ell,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{\ell};V)(\xi_{j\ell})_{11}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(\xi_{j\ell})_{21}\cdot\theta^{\ast}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma s\big(g_{j,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V),\alpha^{j}_{\gamma}\big)+\sqrt{2}\rho_{j},

(78)

where we recall
V=(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0},\rho_{1},\ldots,\rho_{T})
. We note that

\theta\mapsto s(\theta,\alpha_{\gamma}^{j})
is Lipschitz by Assumption

2.2
, so this function
g_{j+1}(\cdot)
is also Lipschitz by the
induction hypothesis. Next, the condition
g_{j+1,2}(\cdot)=\theta^{\ast}

implies

(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{12}=(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{22}=0
for
\ell\leq j
, and
allows us to compute
\mathbf{W}^{j+1}=(\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{1},\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{2})
as
\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{2}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}g_{j+1,1}(\mathbf{U}^{1},\ldots,\mathbf{U}^{j+1};\mathbf{V})-\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\Big(f_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{\ell};\bm{\varepsilon})(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{11}+f_{\ell,2}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{\ell};\bm{\varepsilon})(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{21}\Big)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j+1}_{\gamma}-\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}f_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{\ell};\bm{\varepsilon})(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{11}.

Hence with

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{j+1}_{\gamma}-\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}-\bm{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{1}-\mathbf{W}^{j+1}_{2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}f_{\ell,1}(\mathbf{W}^{0},\ldots,\mathbf{W}^{\ell};\bm{\varepsilon})(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{11}-\bm{\varepsilon},

to satisfy (
77
) we can define
f_{j+1,2}=0
and

\displaystyle f_{j+1,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j+1};\varepsilon)={-}\frac{\beta}{\delta}\Big(W^{j+1}_{1}-W^{j+1}_{2}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}f_{\ell,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{\ell};\varepsilon)(\zeta_{j+1,\ell})_{11}-\varepsilon\Big).

(79)

This is also Lipschitz by the induction hypothesis, completing the induction.
So using (
76
–
77
), the state evolution
(
75
), and the fact that
X_{n}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}X

implies
f(X_{n})\overset{W_{2}}{\to}f(X)
for Lipschitz
f
, we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta^{\ast}_{j},\tilde{\theta}^{0}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\tilde{\theta}^{T}_{\gamma,j}\big)}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0},\ldots,\theta^{T}\big),\quad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\big(\eta^{\ast}_{i},\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{\eta}^{0}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\tilde{\eta}^{T}_{\gamma,i}\big)}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}\big(W^{*},\varepsilon,\eta^{0},\ldots,\eta^{T}\big).

(80)

Here, the laws on the right side are defined by
setting
W^{*}=W_{2}^{i}
for each
i\geq 1
, and

\displaystyle\theta^{i}=g_{i,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V),\quad\eta^{i}={-}\frac{\delta}{\beta}f_{i,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{i};\varepsilon)+W^{*}+\varepsilon,

where
\{U^{i}\}=\{(U^{i}_{1},U^{i}_{2})\}
and
\{W^{i}\}=\{(W^{i}_{1},W^{i}_{2})\}

are the Gaussian processes from AMP state evolution, independent of
\varepsilon,V

with covariance kernels given by (
74
).

Let us now show that

\mathsf{P}(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0},\ldots,\theta^{T})=\mathsf{P}\big(\theta^{\ast},\theta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\gamma}\big),\qquad\mathsf{P}(W^{*},\varepsilon,\eta^{0},\ldots,\eta^{T})=\mathsf{P}\big({-}w_{\gamma}^{\ast},\varepsilon,\eta^{0}_{\gamma},\ldots,\eta^{T}_{\gamma}\big),

(81)

where the laws on the right sides are the variables of the discrete DMFT
equations. This
will conclude the proof of (
71
). To do so, let us
define from the AMP state evolution variables (
80
) the quantities

\begin{gathered}u_{\gamma}^{i}=\delta U_{1}^{i+1},\quad w_{\gamma}^{i}={-}W_{1}^{i},\quad w_{\gamma}^{*}={-}W^{*},\quad\theta_{\gamma}^{i}=\theta^{i},\quad\eta_{\gamma}^{i}=\eta^{i},\\
\frac{\partial\theta^{i}_{\gamma}}{\partial u^{j}_{\gamma}}=\frac{1}{\delta}\frac{\partial g_{i,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{j+1}}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V),\quad\frac{\partial\eta^{i}_{\gamma}}{\partial w^{j}_{\gamma}}=\frac{\delta}{\beta}\frac{\partial f_{i,1}}{\partial W^{j}_{1}}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{i};\varepsilon).\end{gathered}

(82)

Then it suffices to check that these quantities satisfy the discrete DMFT
equations (
57
–
64
),
by uniqueness of the iterative
construction (
65
) of the solution to these
discrete DMFT equations. We first note that by (
74
),

\{u^{j}_{\gamma}\}
and
(w_{\gamma}^{*},\{w_{\gamma}^{j}\})

thus defined are centered Gaussian processes with covariance

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[u_{\gamma}^{i}u_{\gamma}^{j}]

\displaystyle=\delta^{3}\mathbb{E}[f_{i,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{i};\varepsilon)f_{j,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j};\varepsilon)]=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\eta^{i}-W^{\ast}-\varepsilon)(\eta^{j}-W^{\ast}-\varepsilon)],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{\gamma}^{i}w_{\gamma}^{j}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[g_{i,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V)g_{j,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)]=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{i}\theta^{j}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[w_{\gamma}^{i}w_{\gamma}^{*}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[g_{i,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{i};V)g_{0,2}(V)]=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{i}\theta^{*}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(w_{\gamma}^{*})^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[g_{0,2}(V)^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[(\theta^{*})^{2}],

which verifies (
59
) and (
63
)
in light of (
82
).

We next check the recursions (
58
) and
(
62
) for the response:
Recall that the AMP Onsager corrections are

\begin{gathered}(\zeta_{j,s})_{11}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)\Big],\\
(\xi_{j,s})_{11}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\delta\,\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j};\varepsilon)\Big],\quad(\xi_{j,s})_{21}=\mathbb{E}\Big[\delta\,\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{2}}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j};\varepsilon)\Big].\end{gathered}

(83)

By definition of
g_{j,1}
in (
78
), we have
\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}=0
for
s\geq j
,
\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{s+1}}=\gamma\delta
if
s=j-1
, and if
s\leq j-2
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial g_{j-1,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}+\gamma\delta\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-1}\frac{\partial g_{\ell,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}(\xi_{j-1,\ell})_{11}+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(g_{j-1,1},\alpha^{t_{j-1}}_{\gamma})\frac{\partial g_{j-1,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{s+1}}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\gamma\delta(\xi_{j-1,j-1})_{11}+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(g_{j-1,1},\alpha^{t_{j-1}}_{\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial g_{j-1,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{s+1}}+\gamma\delta\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-2}(\xi_{j-1,\ell})_{11}\frac{\partial g_{\ell,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{s+1}}

(84)

where both sides are evaluated at
(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)
.
Similarly, by definition of
f_{j,1}(\cdot)
in (
79
), we have

\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{1}^{s}}=\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{2}^{s}}=0
if
s>j
,
\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{1}^{s}}=-\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{2}^{s}}=-\beta/\delta
if
s=j
, and if
s<j
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}=-\frac{\beta}{\delta}\sum_{\ell=s}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{\ell,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}(\zeta_{j,\ell})_{11}=-\frac{\beta}{\delta}\Big(\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{\ell,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}(\zeta_{j,\ell})_{11}-\frac{\beta}{\delta}(\zeta_{j,s})_{11}\Big),

(85)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{2}}=-\frac{\beta}{\delta}\sum_{\ell=s}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{\ell,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{2}}(\zeta_{j,\ell})_{11}=-\frac{\beta}{\delta}\Big(\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{\ell,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{2}}(\zeta_{j,\ell})_{11}+\frac{\beta}{\delta}(\zeta_{j,s})_{11}\Big).

(86)

Here, these recursions imply that

\{\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}\}
and

\{\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{2}}\}
are deterministic. Under the definitions
(
60
), (
64
),
and (
82
), we have

\displaystyle R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,s)=\frac{1}{\delta}\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j};V)\Big]=\frac{1}{\delta}(\zeta_{j,s})_{11},\quad R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(j,s)=\delta^{2}\Big[\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}\Big]=\delta(\xi_{j,s})_{11}.

(87)

Then by (
84
) and (
85
),
\{\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}\}
and
\{\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}\}
satisfy the recursions

\displaystyle\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\gamma\delta\beta+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(g_{j-1,1};\alpha^{j-1}_{\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial g_{j-1,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}}+\gamma\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-2}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(j-1,\ell)\frac{\partial g_{\ell,1}}{\partial U^{s+1}_{1}},

\displaystyle\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}

\displaystyle=-\beta\Big(\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{j-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,\ell)\frac{\partial f_{\ell,1}}{\partial W^{s}_{1}}-\frac{\beta}{\delta}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,s)\Big),

which verify (
58
) and (
62
) in view of
(
82
) and above boundary conditions

\frac{\partial g_{j,1}}{\partial U_{1}^{j}}=\gamma\delta

and
\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{1}^{j}}=-\beta/\delta
.

Finally we check the primary recursions
(
57
) and (
61
).
By (
82
),
definition of
g_{j+1,1}(\cdot)
in (
78
), and

(\xi_{j,j})_{11}={-}(\xi_{j,j})_{21}=-\beta
,
we have

\displaystyle\theta^{j+1}

\displaystyle=g_{j+1,1}(U^{1},\ldots,U^{j+1};V)

\displaystyle=\theta^{j}+\gamma\delta\Big(U^{j+1}_{1}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}\theta^{\ell}(\xi_{j,\ell})_{11}+\sum_{\ell=0}^{j}(\xi_{j,\ell})_{21}\cdot\theta^{\ast}\Big)+\gamma s\big(\theta^{j},\alpha^{j}_{\gamma}\big)+\sqrt{2}\rho_{j}

\displaystyle=\theta^{j}-\gamma\delta\beta(\theta^{j}-\theta^{\ast})+\gamma s\big(\theta^{j},\alpha^{j}_{\gamma}\big)+\gamma\delta\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}\theta^{\ell}(\xi_{j,\ell})_{11}+\gamma\delta\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}\theta^{*}(\xi_{j,\ell})_{21}+\gamma\delta U^{j+1}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\rho_{j}.

(88)

Similarly, by definition of
f_{j,1}(\cdot)
in (
79
) and

W^{i}_{2}\equiv W^{\ast}
,

\displaystyle\eta^{j}

\displaystyle={-}\frac{\delta}{\beta}f_{j,1}(W^{0},\ldots,W^{j};\varepsilon)+W^{*}+\varepsilon={-}\frac{\beta}{\delta}\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}(\zeta_{j,\ell})_{11}(\eta^{\ell}-W^{\ast}-\varepsilon)+W^{j}_{1}.

(89)

Applying (
83
), note that in (
88
) we have

A_{j}:=\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}(\xi_{j,\ell})_{21}=\delta\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{j,1}}{\partial W_{2}^{s}}
.
Then by the recursion (
86
) and first identification of
(
87
), we have

\displaystyle A_{j}=-\beta\delta\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}\sum_{s=\ell}^{j-1}\frac{\partial f_{s,1}}{\partial W^{\ell}_{2}}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,s)=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{j-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,s)\delta\sum_{\ell=0}^{s}\frac{\partial f_{s,1}}{\partial W^{\ell}_{2}}=-\beta\sum_{s=0}^{j-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(j,s)(A_{s}+\beta).

This coincides with the recursion for
\{\beta\frac{\partial\eta_{\gamma}^{j}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{\ast}}\}
in (
69
).
Hence
A_{j}=\beta\frac{\partial\eta^{j}_{\gamma}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{\ast}}=\frac{1}{\delta}R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(j,\ast)={-}\frac{1}{\delta}\sum_{s=0}^{j-1}R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(j,s)
, where the last step applies
Lemma
4.2
. Applying this form of
A_{j}

and (
87
), we may write the equations
(
88
–
89
) as

\displaystyle\theta^{j+1}

\displaystyle=\theta^{j}-\gamma\delta\beta(\theta^{j}-\theta^{\ast})+\gamma s\big(\theta^{j},\alpha^{j}_{\gamma}\big)+\gamma\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}R^{\gamma}_{\eta}(j,\ell)(\theta^{\ell}-\theta^{\ast})+\gamma\delta U^{j+1}_{1}+\sqrt{2}\rho_{j},

\displaystyle\eta^{j}

\displaystyle=-\beta\sum_{\ell=0}^{j-1}R^{\gamma}_{\theta}(j,\ell)(\eta^{\ell}-W^{\ast}-\varepsilon)+W^{j}_{1},

which verifies (
57
) and (
61
)
in view of (
82
).
This verifies that the definitions (
82
) indeed satisfy
(
57
–
64
),
concluding the proof of (
71
).

Step 2: Comparison with auxiliary dynamics.
Let us now prove
(
66
–
68
)
for the original dynamics with an adaptive drift parameter

\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}
.
We will prove via induction that, almost surely as
n,d\rightarrow\infty
,
for each
t=0,\ldots,T
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}\rightarrow 0,\qquad\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}\rightarrow\alpha^{t}_{\gamma}.

(90)

Since

\displaystyle W_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta^{\ast}_{j},\theta^{0}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\theta^{T}_{\gamma,j}\big)},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta^{\ast}_{j},\tilde{\theta}^{0}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\tilde{\theta}^{T}_{\gamma,j}\big)}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{1}{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}

(91)

\displaystyle W_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\big(\eta^{\ast}_{i},\varepsilon_{i},\eta^{0}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\eta^{T}_{\gamma,i}\big)},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\big(\eta^{\ast}_{i},\varepsilon_{i},\tilde{\eta}^{0}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\tilde{\eta}^{T}_{\gamma,i},\big)}\Big)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\lVert\bm{\eta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\eta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}

and
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}
is almost surely bounded for all large
n,d
,
the above inductive claim together with (
71
) implies
(
66
–
68
).

The base case of
t=0
in (
90
) holds exactly. Suppose
(
90
) holds up to time
t
. For
t+1
, we see that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t+1}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t+1}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\Big((1+\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{4})\frac{1}{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\lVert s(\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma})-s(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma},\alpha^{t}_{\gamma})\rVert^{2}\Big).

Applying boundedness of
\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and Lipschitz continuity of
s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2
, we have by the induction hypothesis
that
\frac{1}{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t+1}_{\gamma}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t+1}_{\gamma}\rVert^{2}\rightarrow 0
almost surely. Next, we have by the Lipschitz continuity of
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.3
that

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1}_{\gamma}-\alpha_{\gamma}^{t+1}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}-\alpha_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert+\gamma\left\|\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta^{t}_{\gamma,\ell}}\Big)-\mathcal{G}(\alpha^{t}_{\gamma},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{t}_{\gamma}))\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}-\alpha_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert+CW_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta^{t}_{\gamma,\ell}},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{t}_{\gamma})\Big),

which converges almost surely to 0 by the induction hypothesis and the above
implication (
91
). This establishes the induction for (
90
) and hence completes the proof.
∎

4.2
Step 2: Discretization error of DMFT
equation

We now define a piecewise constant embedding of the components of the
discrete DMFT system
(
57
–
64
) into continuous
time, and show that this converges to the solution of the continuous DMFT system
established in Theorem
2.4
, in the limit
\gamma\to 0
.

For all times
t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, define

\displaystyle\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor=\max\{i\gamma:i\gamma\leq t,i\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}\}\in\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad\left\lceil t\right\rceil=\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor+\gamma\in\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+},\quad[t]=\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor/\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}.

(92)

Fixing
T>0
, let
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
be the space of functions

(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})\equiv\{(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s),\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s),\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}
that are piecewise constant
and right-continuous in
(s,t)
with jumps at
\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, i.e.
\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor,\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor)
for all

0\leq s\leq t\leq T
and similarly for
\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
. Analogously, let
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
be the space of functions

(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})\equiv\{\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}
that are piecewise
constant and right-continuous with jumps at
\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.

We define a map
\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{\eta\to\theta}:\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\to\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
as follows: Given
X^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
, let
\{\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a mean-zero
Gaussian process with covariance
\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
,
let
\{b^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a standard Brownian motion, and define
processes
\{\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

and
\{\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}\}_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}
by

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big[{-}\delta\beta(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma}-\theta^{\ast})+s(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}^{s}_{\gamma})+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\eta}(s,r)(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{r}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}r+\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}b^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor},

(93)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=1+\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bigg[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{r},\bar{\alpha}^{r}_{\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}+\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor r\right\rfloor}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(r,r^{\prime})\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{r^{\prime}}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\bigg]\mathrm{d}r.

(94)

Define from these processes

\begin{gathered}\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma}\Big],\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,\ast)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}\theta^{\ast}\Big],\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(*,*)=\mathbb{E}[(\theta^{*})^{2}],\\
\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}\Big],\quad\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\alpha^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\mathcal{G}(\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s}))\mathrm{d}s\end{gathered}

(95)

and set
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})
. We also define a map

\mathcal{T}^{\gamma}_{\theta\to\eta}:\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\to\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
as follows: Given
Y^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
,
let
(\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{*},\{\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})
be a mean-zero
Gaussian process with covariance
\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
, and define

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}^{t}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle={-}\beta\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)(\bar{\eta}^{s}_{\gamma}+\bar{w}^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}s-\bar{w}^{t}_{\gamma},

(96)

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{s}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\beta\bar{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\beta\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,r)\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{r}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{s}_{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}r.

(97)

Define from these processes

\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\eta}(t,s)=\delta\beta\,\mathbb{E}\Big[\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{s}_{\gamma}}\Big],\quad\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}\Big[(\bar{\eta}^{t}_{\gamma}+\bar{w}^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)(\bar{\eta}^{s}_{\gamma}+\bar{w}^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)\Big],\quad\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}=\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}

(98)

and set
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma})=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})
. These maps may be understood as discrete
approximations of
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}

constructed in Section
3.2
, with domains restricted to
the spaces
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
of piecewise constant
inputs.

Recall the spaces
\mathcal{S}_{\theta},\mathcal{S}_{\eta},\mathcal{S}
of Section
3.1
.
The following lemma shows that the above maps

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma},\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
are well-defined,
that the unique fixed point of these maps is a
piecewise constant embedding of the discrete-time
DMFT system in Section
4.1
, and that furthermore
this fixed point belongs to
\mathcal{S}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

(a)

Given any
X^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
and realization of
\theta^{*}
,
\theta^{0}
,
\{b^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, and
\{\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
the processes (
93
–
94
) have a unique solution,
and this solution is piecewise constant and right-continuous with jumps at

\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Consequently,

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
is a well-defined map from

\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
to
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
.

(b)

Given any
Y^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
and realization of
\varepsilon
and

(\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{*},\{\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{t}\})
,
the processes (
96
–
97
) have a unique solution,
and this solution is piecewise constant and right-continuous with jumps at

\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
. Consequently,
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
is a well-defined
map from
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
to
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
.

(c)

The map
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}\circ\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
has a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
, and
the map
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}^{\gamma}=\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}\circ\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
has a unique fixed point in
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
.
These fixed points are given precisely by

\begin{gathered}\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}=\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\alpha_{\gamma}^{[t]},\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=C_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t],[s]),\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,*)=C_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t],*),\quad\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=C_{\eta}^{\gamma}([t],[s]),\\
\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\begin{cases}1&\text{ if }[s]=[t]\\
\frac{1}{\gamma}\,R_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t],[s])&\text{ if }[s]<[t],\end{cases}\quad\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\begin{cases}\delta\beta^{2}&\text{ if }[s]=[t]\\
\frac{1}{\gamma}\,R_{\eta}^{\gamma}([t],[s])&\text{ if }[s]<[t]\end{cases}\end{gathered}

(99)

for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
, where

(\alpha_{\gamma},C_{\theta}^{\gamma},C_{\eta}^{\gamma},R_{\theta}^{\gamma},R_{\eta}^{\gamma})

are the components of the discrete DMFT system defined iteratively in time
via (
60
) and (
64
).

(d)

For any
\gamma>0
sufficiently small,
we have
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}(\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta})\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
,

\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}(\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta})\subseteq\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
, and the fixed point
(
99
) belongs to
\mathcal{S}
.

Proof.

For (a), if
X^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
, then
\{\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
is also piecewise constant and
right-continuous by these properties of
\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
. Then
an easy induction on
k
shows that
(
93
) has a unique solution over

t\in[0,k\gamma)
for each integer
k\geq 1
, which is given by

\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}=\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}
. By definition,
(
94
) is given by
\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}}=1
for all
s\geq 0
and

t\in[s,\left\lceil s\right\rceil)
. Then for each
s\geq 0
, an induction on
k

shows also that (
94
) has a unique
solution on
[s,\left\lceil s\right\rceil+k\gamma)
for each integer
k\geq 1
,
which is given by
\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}}=\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}}
, and furthermore this solution depends on
s
only via
\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor
,
i.e.
\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}}=\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}}
.
Thus the solutions of
(
93
–
94
)
are piecewise constant and right-continuous,
implying the same properties for
\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}
defined by (
95
).
This shows (a).

Part (b) follows from analogous inductive arguments, using that if

Y^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
, then
\{\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
is also
piecewise constant and right-continuous, and hence so are
\{\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
and
\{\frac{\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}}{\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{s}}\}
.

Part (c) also follows by induction: Since any fixed point is piecewise constant,
it suffices to consider the values at
\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
By (
93
),

\bar{\theta}^{0}_{\gamma}=\theta^{0}
. Then by (
95
),

\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,0)=1,\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,0)=\mathbb{E}[(\theta^{0})^{2}]=C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,0),\quad\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,*)=\mathbb{E}[\theta^{0}\theta^{*}]=C_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,*),\quad\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{0}=0.

Then by (
96
),

\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{0}={-}\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{0}
,
so
(\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{0},\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{*})
is equal in joint law
to the discrete DMFT variables
(\eta_{\gamma}^{0},w_{\gamma}^{*})
. Then
by (
98
), any fixed point must satisfy

\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(0,0)=\delta\beta^{2},\quad\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(0,0)=\delta\beta^{2}\mathbb{E}[(\eta_{\gamma}^{0}+w_{\gamma}^{*}-\varepsilon)^{2}]=C_{\eta}(0,0),\quad\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{0}=0.

Suppose inductively that there is a unique fixed point over times

s\leq t
in
\{0,\gamma,\ldots,k\gamma\}
, and
consider now
t=(k+1)\gamma
. The equations
(
93
–
94
)
and the piecewise constant nature of all processes imply

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}

\displaystyle=\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}+\int_{k\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}\Big[{-}\delta\beta(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma}-\theta^{\ast})+s(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}^{s}_{\gamma})+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\eta}(s,r)(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{r}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}r+\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}(b^{(k+1)\gamma}-b^{k\gamma})

\displaystyle=\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}+\gamma\Big({-}\delta\beta(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}-\theta^{*})+s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma})+\gamma\sum_{\ell=0}^{k-1}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(k\gamma,\ell\gamma)(\bar{\theta}^{\ell\gamma}_{\gamma}-\theta^{*})+\bar{u}^{k\gamma}_{\gamma}\Big)+\sqrt{2}(b^{(k+1)\gamma}-b^{k\gamma}),

\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}}=1
, and for any
j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}+\int_{k\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}\bigg[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{r},\bar{\alpha}^{r}_{\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{r}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}}+\int_{(j+1)\gamma}^{k\gamma}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(r,r^{\prime})\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{r^{\prime}}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}\bigg]\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}+\gamma\bigg[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma})\Big)\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}+\gamma\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{k-1}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(k\gamma,\ell\gamma)\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{\ell\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}\bigg]

Comparing these equations with
(
57
–
58
) and
applying
\gamma\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(k\gamma,\ell\gamma)=R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(k,\ell)
,

\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma}=\alpha_{\gamma}^{k}
, and the equality in law

(\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{0},\ldots,\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{k\gamma})\overset{L}{=}(u_{\gamma}^{0},\ldots,u_{\gamma}^{k})
by the induction hypothesis, this shows the
equality in law

\{\theta^{*},\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{i\gamma},\tfrac{\partial\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{i\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}\}_{i<j\leq k+1}\overset{L}{=}\{\theta^{*},\theta_{\gamma}^{i},\gamma^{-1}\tfrac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{i}}{\partial u_{\gamma}^{j}}\}_{i<j\leq k+1}.

Then (
99
) holds for the components

\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}
of any fixed
point up to times

s\leq t
in
\{0,\gamma,\ldots,(k+1)\gamma\}
.

Now the equations
(
96
–
97
)
imply

\displaystyle\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}

\displaystyle={-}\beta\int_{0}^{(k+1)\gamma}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,s)(\bar{\eta}^{s}_{\gamma}+\bar{w}^{\ast}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}s-\bar{w}^{(k+1)\gamma}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle={-}\beta\gamma\sum_{j=0}^{k}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,j\gamma)\big(\bar{\eta}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}+\bar{w}^{*}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon\big)-\bar{w}^{(k+1)\gamma}_{\gamma},

\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{(k+1)\gamma}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{(k+1)\gamma}_{\gamma}}=\delta\beta^{2}
, and for all
j\leq k
,

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{(k+1)\gamma}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=\beta\bar{R}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,j\gamma)-\beta\int_{(j+1)\gamma}^{(k+1)\gamma}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,r)\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{r}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\beta\bar{R}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,j\gamma)-\beta\gamma\sum_{\ell=j+1}^{k}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}((k+1)\gamma,\ell\gamma)\frac{\partial\bar{\eta}^{\ell\gamma}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}^{j\gamma}_{\gamma}}.

Comparing these equations with
(
61
–
62
) and
applying again the induction hypothesis, this shows the equality in law

\{\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{i\gamma},\tfrac{\partial\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{i\gamma}}{\partial\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{j\gamma}}\}_{i<j\leq k+1}\overset{L}{=}\{\eta_{\gamma}^{i},\gamma^{-1}\tfrac{\partial\eta_{\gamma}^{i}}{\partial w_{\gamma}^{j}}\}_{i<j\leq k+1}.

Then (
99
) also holds for the components

\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}
of any fixed point
up to times
s\leq t
in
\{0,\gamma,\ldots,(k+1)\gamma\}
,
completing the induction. Thus any fixed points of
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}^{\gamma}

and
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
must satisfy
(
99
) for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,
implying also that such fixed points are unique in
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
and

\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
by uniqueness of the iterative
construction (
65
) of the solution to the
discrete DMFT equations. This shows (c).

For (d), we check that
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
define contractive mappings on

\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
.
Note that if
Y^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}

and
X^{\gamma}=\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma})=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})
, then
setting
\bar{\xi}_{\gamma}^{t}=\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}+\bar{w}^{*}_{\gamma}-\varepsilon
, the
same arguments as in Lemma
3.5
show

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}(\bar{\xi}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2\Big[\beta^{2}\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}(t-s+1)^{2}\cdot\Phi_{R_{\theta}}^{2}(t-s)\mathbb{E}(\bar{\xi}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s+2\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)+2\tau_{*}^{2}+\sigma^{2}\Big],

\displaystyle|\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)|

\displaystyle\leq|\beta|\Big(\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s^{\prime})|\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s)|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\delta|\beta|\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)\Big).

Upper-bounding these integrals
\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}
and

\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}
by
\int_{0}^{t}
and
\int_{s}^{t}
, we obtain as in
Lemma
3.5
that

\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,t)\leq\Phi_{C_{\gamma}}(t)
and

\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)\leq\Phi_{R_{\gamma}}(t-s)
.
All continuity conditions
defining
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
are automatically satisfied since the components of

X^{\gamma}
are piecewise constant outside the knots
D=[0,T]\cap\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Thus
X^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
, i.e.
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
maps
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
into

\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
.

Conversely, suppose

X^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha})\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
, and let

Y^{\gamma}=\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}})
. A small extension of the argument
in Lemma
3.6
shows
Y^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
. Let us
explain this extension for
\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}
: Defining

\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t}={-}\delta\beta(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}-\theta^{*})+s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t})+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s+\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t},

we have
\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}=\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}+\gamma\cdot\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t}+\sqrt{2}(b^{t+\gamma}-b^{t})
for
t\in\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Then, analogous to the calculation using Ito’s formula in
Lemma
3.6
, for sufficiently small
\gamma>0
,

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+\gamma,t+\gamma)-\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,t)

\displaystyle=2\gamma\mathbb{E}\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t}+\gamma^{2}\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}+2\gamma

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma}{1-\gamma}\mathbb{E}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}+[\gamma(1-\gamma)+\gamma^{2}]\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}+2\gamma

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\Big(1.1\mathbb{E}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{t})^{2}+2\Big)=\int_{t}^{t+\gamma}\Big(1.1\mathbb{E}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}+2\Big)\mathrm{d}s,

the last equality holding because
\bar{v}_{\gamma}
and
\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}
are
piecewise constant. Summing this inequality shows

\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,t)-\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(0,0)\leq\int_{0}^{t}\Big(1.1\mathbb{E}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}+\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}+2\Big)\mathrm{d}s

for all
t\in[0,T]
. Then,
bounding
\mathbb{E}(\bar{v}_{\gamma}^{s})^{2}
as in (
44
) of
Lemma
3.6
, this shows that

\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,t)\leq\Phi_{C_{\theta}}(t)
. Similar extensions of the
arguments in Lemma
3.6

show that
\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)\leq\Phi_{R_{\theta}}(t-s)

and
\|\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}\|^{2}\leq\Phi_{\alpha}(t)
, so

Y^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
as claimed. Then

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
maps
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
into

\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
.

The same argument as in Lemmas
3.7
and

3.8
bound the moduli-of-continuity of

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
and

\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
in the metrics
d(\cdot)
of
Section
3.2
, implying that

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
and
\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
are
contractive for sufficiently large
\lambda>0
defining
d(\cdot)
.
These metrics induce the topologies of uniform
convergence on the spaces
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}

and
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
, which are equivalent to closed subsets of finite-dimensional vector spaces and hence also complete. Then
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}^{\gamma}
and

\mathcal{T}_{\theta\to\theta}^{\gamma}
have unique fixed points
in
\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
and
\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}

by the Banach fixed-point theorem. These must coincide with the fixed point
(
99
), by the uniqueness statement (without
restriction to
\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
and
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
) shown in part (c). Thus
this fixed point (
99
) belongs to
\mathcal{S}
.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

There exists a constant
C>0
(depending on
T
but not on
\lambda,\gamma
)
such that for all large enough
\lambda>0

defining the metrics (
47
) and all sufficiently small
\gamma>0
,

(a)

For any
X^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
,

d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}(X^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\big)\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}.

(b)

For any
Y^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
,

d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma})\big)\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}.

Proof.

We show part (a). Consider any

X^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})
, and
denote

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}(X^{\gamma})=(R_{\theta},C_{\theta},\tilde{\alpha})

and
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})
. Throughout,
C,C^{\prime}>0
denote constants
depending on
T
but not on
\lambda,\gamma

and changing from instance to instance.

Bound of
d(C_{\theta},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma})
.

Given
X^{\gamma}
, let us couple the evolutions
(
12
) and (
93
)
by a common realization of
\{\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
with covariance
\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
and a common Brownian motion. Then
by definition, we have

\displaystyle\theta^{t}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[-\delta\beta(\theta^{s}-\theta^{*})+s(\theta^{s},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s})+\int_{0}^{s}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,b^{t},

\displaystyle\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}

\displaystyle=\theta^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big[-\delta\beta(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma}-\theta^{\ast})+s(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}^{s}_{\gamma})+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\bar{R}^{\gamma}_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,b^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}.

Then
\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}\leq 6[(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)+(V)+(VI)]
where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\delta\beta(\theta^{s}-\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma})\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}(s(\theta^{s},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s})-s(\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s})\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta^{s^{\prime}}-\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\int_{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}^{s}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(V)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big(\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\Big[-\delta\beta(\theta^{s}-\theta^{*})+s(\theta^{s},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s})+\int_{0}^{s}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{\ast})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{s}\Big]\mathrm{d}s\Big)^{2},

\displaystyle(VI)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}(\sqrt{2}\,b^{t}-\sqrt{2}\,b^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor})^{2}.

By the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
,
using the Lipschitz continuity of
s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2
,
we may show

(I)+(II)+(III)\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}e^{2\lambda t}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda s}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{s}-\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma})^{2}.

Applying
s-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor\leq\gamma
,
t-\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\leq\gamma
,
and the bounds for
\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}
implied by
X^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}
, we have

(IV)+(V)+(VI)\leq C\gamma.

Then
