\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-2\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}+C\gamma,

and choosing large enough
\lambda>0
yields

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}}\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\gamma}.

(100)

This implies as in the proof of Lemma
3.7

that
d(C_{\theta},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma})\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\gamma}
.

Bound of
d(R_{\theta},\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma})
.
Denote by

r_{\theta}(t,s)=\frac{\partial\theta^{t}}{\partial u^{s}}
and

\bar{r}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\frac{\partial\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}}{\partial\bar{u}^{s}_{\gamma}}
the processes (
13
) and
(
94
) defined from the above coupling of

\{\theta^{t}\}
and
\{\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
. Then by definition, we have

\displaystyle r_{\theta}(t,s)

\displaystyle=1+\int_{s}^{t}\bigg[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\theta^{s^{\prime}},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}})\Big)r_{\theta}(s^{\prime},s)+\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})r_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime},

\displaystyle\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)

\displaystyle=1+\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bigg[\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\bar{\theta}^{s^{\prime}}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}})\Big)\bar{r}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s)+\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor s^{\prime}\right\rfloor}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\bigg]\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

Hence
\mathbb{E}|r_{\theta}(t,s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)|\leq(I)+(II)+(III)+(IV)
where

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big|\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\theta^{s^{\prime}},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}})\Big)r_{\theta}(s^{\prime},s)-\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\bar{\theta}^{s^{\prime}}_{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}^{s^{\prime}}_{\gamma})\Big)\bar{r}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s)\Big|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg],

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor s^{\prime}\right\rfloor}\Big|\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})(r_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s))\Big|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg],

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\bigg[\mathbf{1}\{\left\lceil s\right\rceil\leq\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\}\int_{\left\lceil s\right\rceil}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\int_{(s,\left\lceil s\right\rceil)\cup(\left\lfloor s^{\prime}\right\rfloor,s^{\prime})}\Big|\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})r_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s)\Big|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\,\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}\bigg],

\displaystyle(IV)

\displaystyle=\int_{(s,\left\lceil s\right\rceil)\cup(\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor,t)}\bigg|\Big({-}\delta\beta+\partial_{\theta}s(\theta^{s^{\prime}},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{s^{\prime}})\Big)r_{\theta}(s^{\prime},s)+\int_{s}^{s^{\prime}}\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(s^{\prime},s^{\prime\prime})r_{\theta}(s^{\prime\prime},s)\mathrm{d}s^{\prime\prime}\bigg|\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}.

By the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
,
using the above bound (
100
) and Lipschitz continuity of

\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2
, we may show

(I)+(II)\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\Big(\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}|r_{\theta}(t,s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)|\Big)+C\sqrt{\gamma}.

Applying
\left\lceil s\right\rceil-s\leq\gamma
,
s^{\prime}-\left\lfloor s^{\prime}\right\rfloor\leq\gamma
, and
t-\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\leq\gamma
, we have

(III)+(IV)\leq C\gamma.

Then

\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}|r_{\theta}(t,s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)|\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}|r_{\theta}(t,s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)|+C\sqrt{\gamma},

and choosing large enough
\lambda>0
and rearranging gives

\displaystyle d(R_{\theta},\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma})\leq\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}|r_{\theta}(t,s)-\bar{r}^{\gamma}_{\theta}(t,s)|\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}.

Bound of
d(\tilde{\alpha},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})
.
By definition,

\tilde{\alpha}^{t}=\alpha^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\alpha}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{s}))\mathrm{d}s,\qquad\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\alpha^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\mathcal{G}(\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s}))\mathrm{d}s,

so
\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|\leq(I)+(II)
where

(I)=\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big\|\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\alpha}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{s}))-\mathcal{G}(\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{s}))\Big\|\mathrm{d}s,\qquad(II)=\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\Big\|\mathcal{G}(\tilde{\alpha}^{s},\mathsf{P}(\theta^{s}))\Big\|\mathrm{d}s.

By the same arguments as in the proof of Lemma
3.7
,
using the above bound (
100
)
and the Lipschitz continuity of
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
in Assumption

2.3
, we have

(I)\leq\frac{C}{\lambda}e^{\lambda t}\sup_{s\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda s}\|\tilde{\alpha}^{s}-\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s}\|\,\mathrm{d}s+C\sqrt{\gamma}

Using
t-\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\leq\gamma
, we have
(II)\leq C\gamma
. So choosing

\lambda>0
large enough and rearranging shows

d(\tilde{\alpha},\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma})=\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\|\tilde{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\tilde{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}.

This concludes the proof of (a). The proof of (b) is analogous, and we omit this
for brevity.
∎

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,
\{\eta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
, and

\{\alpha^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be the components of the solution to the DMFT
system in Theorem
2.4
, and let

\{\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
,

\{\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be defined from the components of
the fixed point (
99
) via
(
93
) and (
96
).
Then for any fixed
m\geq 0
and
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in[0,T]
,
as
\gamma\to 0
,

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\bar{\theta}^{t_{1}}_{\gamma},\ldots,\bar{\theta}^{t_{m}}_{\gamma})

\displaystyle\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t_{1}},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}})

(101)

\displaystyle\mathsf{P}(\bar{w}_{\gamma}^{*},\varepsilon,\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t_{1}},\ldots,\bar{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t_{m}})

\displaystyle\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(w^{*},\varepsilon,\eta^{t_{1}},\ldots,\eta^{t_{m}})

(102)

\displaystyle\{\bar{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\{\alpha^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

(103)

where (
103
) holds in the sense of uniform
convergence on
C([0,T],\mathbb{R}^{K})
.

Proof.

Let
X^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})

and
Y^{\gamma}=(\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{C}_{\theta}^{\gamma},\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma})

be the components of the fixed point (
99
),
and let
X=(R_{\eta},C_{\eta},\alpha)
and
Y=(R_{\theta},C_{\theta},\alpha)

be those of the unique solution to the continuous DMFT system prescribed by
Theorem
2.4
.
Let
d(\cdot)
denote the metrics introduced in (
48
) and
(
49
), for a sufficiently large choice of
\lambda>0
.
By Lemma
4.3
,

X^{\gamma}\in\mathcal{D}_{\eta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\eta}

for all sufficiently small
\gamma>0
, so
\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}(X^{\gamma})
is
well-defined. Then, applying the fixed point conditions
for
X^{\gamma}
and
X
,

\displaystyle d(X,X^{\gamma})=d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\big)\leq d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X^{\gamma})\big)+d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\big).

By Lemmas
3.7
and
3.8
,

\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\eta}
is a contraction on
\mathcal{S}_{\eta}

for large enough
\lambda>0
, for which
the first term satisfies
d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X^{\gamma})\big)\leq\frac{1}{2}d(X,X^{\gamma})
.
Thus, rearranging shows

d(X,X^{\gamma})\leq 2d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}(X^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\big)=2d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}\circ\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}(X^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}\circ\mathcal{T}_{\eta\to\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\big).

Letting
Y^{\prime}=\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}(X^{\gamma})\in\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
and

Y^{\gamma}=\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma})\in\mathcal{D}_{\theta}^{\gamma}\cap\mathcal{S}_{\theta}
, this shows

d(X,X^{\gamma})\leq 2d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\prime}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma})\big)\leq 2d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\prime}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\gamma})\big)+2d\big(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma})\big).

By Lemma
4.4
,
d(Y^{\prime},Y^{\gamma})=d(\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}(X^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\eta\rightarrow\theta}^{\gamma}(X^{\gamma}))\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}
. Then by Lemma

3.8
, the first term is bounded as

d(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\prime}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\gamma}))\leq Cd(Y^{\prime},Y^{\gamma})\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\gamma}
.
By Lemma
4.4
, the second term is also bounded as

d(\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}(Y^{\gamma}),\mathcal{T}_{\theta\rightarrow\eta}^{\gamma}(Y^{\gamma}))\leq C\sqrt{\gamma}
. So combining
these statements and taking
\gamma\to 0
shows

\lim_{\gamma\to 0}d(X,X^{\gamma})=0,\qquad\lim_{\gamma\to 0}d(Y,Y^{\gamma})=0.

(104)

By definition of the metrics
d(\cdot)
, the
convergence (
104
) implies the uniform convergence
statement (
103
). It also implies

\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\sup_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T}|C_{\eta}(t,s)-\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)|=0.

We recall that Theorem
2.4
shows

(R_{\eta},C_{\eta},\alpha)\in\mathcal{S}_{\eta}^{\text{cont}}
, for which
the continuity property (
32
) holds for all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
. Then there exists a coupling of
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\{\bar{u}^{t}_{\gamma}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
with covariance kernels
C_{\eta}(t,s)
and
\bar{C}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
for which

\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}(u^{t}-\bar{u}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}=0,

see e.g.
[
52
, Lemma D.1]
. Defining
\{\theta^{t}\}
and
\{\bar{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
by this coupling of
\{u^{t}\}
and
\{\bar{u}_{\gamma}^{t}\}

and a common Brownian motion, the same arguments as leading
to (
100
) shows

\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{-\lambda t}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}=0,

hence in particular
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\mathbb{E}(\theta^{t}-\bar{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})^{2}=0

for each fixed
t\in[0,T]
under this coupling, which implies
(
101
). A similar argument shows
(
102
).
∎

4.3
Step 3: Discretization of Langevin
dynamics

We now consider a piecewise constant embedding

\{\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma},\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}_{\gamma}\}_{t\in[0,T]}

of the discretized Langevin process
(
55
–
56
), defined as

\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}=\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{[t]},\qquad\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}_{\gamma}=\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{[t]},\qquad\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{X}\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}

where
[t]\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is as previously defined in (
92
).
A simple induction shows that this is equivalently the solution to a modification of the
dynamics (
4
–
5
),

\displaystyle\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big[{-}\beta\,\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{s}-\mathbf{y})+\big(s(\bar{\theta}_{\gamma,j}^{s},\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s})\big)_{j=1}^{d}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor},

(105)

\displaystyle\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\mathcal{G}\Big(\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\bar{\theta}_{\gamma,j}^{s}}\Big)\mathrm{d}s.

We compare this to the solution

\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
of the original
dynamics (
4
–
5
), with

\bm{\eta}^{t}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}
, to show the following lemma.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\bm{\eta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}
be defined by
(
4
–
5
), and let

\{\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}

be defined by the piecewise constant process (
105
).
Then for any fixed
m\geq 1
and
t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in[0,T]
,
there exists a function
\iota:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}

satisfying
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\iota(\gamma)=0
such that
almost surely

\displaystyle\limsup_{n,d\rightarrow\infty}W_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta^{t_{1}}_{j},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}}_{j})},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\bar{\theta}^{t_{1}}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{\gamma,j}^{t_{m}})}\Big)<\iota(\gamma)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n,d\rightarrow\infty}W_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\eta_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i},\eta^{t_{1}}_{i},\ldots,\eta^{t_{m}}_{i})},\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{(\eta_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i},\bar{\eta}^{t_{1}}_{\gamma,i},\ldots,\bar{\eta}_{\gamma,i}^{t_{m}})}\Big)<\iota(\gamma)

\displaystyle\limsup_{n,d\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\big\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\big\rVert<\iota(\gamma).

We proceed to prove Lemma
4.6
.

Lemma 4.7
.

Let
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a standard Brownian motion on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For any fixed
T>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
depending on
T
such that
almost surely

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}\rVert\leq C,\quad\limsup_{d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\big(\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert+\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lceil t\right\rceil}\rVert\big)\leq C\sqrt{\gamma\max(\log(1/\gamma),1)}.

Proof.

We first show that for any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
a\leq b
, we have

\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[a,b]}d^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert\geq u)\leq\exp\big({-}\frac{cdu^{2}}{b-a}\big)
for any
u\geq\sqrt{4(b-a)}
and some constant
c>0
. To see
this, for any
\lambda\in(0,\frac{d}{2(b-a)})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[a,b]}d^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert\geq u)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[a,b]}\exp(\lambda\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert^{2}/d)\geq\exp(\lambda u^{2}))

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(*)}}{{\leq}}e^{-\lambda u^{2}}\mathbb{E}[\exp(\lambda\lVert\mathbf{b}^{b}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert^{2}/d)]\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(**)}}{{=}}e^{-\lambda u^{2}}(1-2\lambda(b-a)/d)^{-d/2},

where
(*)
applies Doob’s maximal inequality for the nonnegative submartingale
\{\exp(\lambda\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert^{2}/d)\}_{t\in[a,b]}
, and
(**)

applies the moment generating function of the
\chi^{2}

distribution. Choosing
\lambda=cd/(b-a)
for some small enough
c>0

and applying
(1-x)\geq e^{-2x}
for small
x>0
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[a,b]}d^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{a}\rVert\geq u)\leq\exp\Big({-}\frac{cdu^{2}}{b-a}+2cd\Big)\leq\exp\Big({-}\frac{cdu^{2}}{2(b-a)}\Big)

for
u\geq\sqrt{4(b-a)}
, proving the inequality. For the first claim, we
apply this with
a=0
,
b=T
,
u=\sqrt{4T}
to yield that

\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}\rVert\geq\sqrt{4T})\leq\exp(-2cd)
,
so the claim follows by the Borel Cantelli lemma. For the second claim, let
N=T/\gamma
(assumed without loss of generality to be an integer greater than 1), and
I_{i}=[(i-1)\gamma,i\gamma)
for
i\in[N]
. Then applying the inequality over these intervals yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(d\gamma)^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert\geq u)\leq N\max_{i\leq N}\mathbb{P}\Big(\sup_{t\in I_{i}}(d\gamma)^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{i\gamma}\rVert\geq u\Big)\leq Ne^{-cdu^{2}}

for any
u\geq 2
. Hence by choosing
u=C\sqrt{\log N}
for large enough

C>0
, we have
\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(d\gamma)^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert\geq C\sqrt{\log N})\leq\exp(-c^{\prime}d)
. A similar argument applies to
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(d\gamma)^{-1/2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lceil t\right\rceil}\rVert
, proving the second claim.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

For any fixed
T>0
, there exists a constant

C>0
depending on
T
such that almost surely

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert/\sqrt{d}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert)\leq C.

Proof.

Let
C>0
denote a constant depending on
T
and changing from instance to
instance. Since

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}^{0}-\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s}-\mathbf{y})-s(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\Big)\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\mathbf{b}^{t},

and
\lVert s(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\rVert\leq C(\sqrt{d}+\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\rVert)
by Assumption
2.2
, we have for every
t\in[0,T]
that

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert\leq\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert+C(\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}+1)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\rVert\Big)\mathrm{d}s+C(\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\rVert+\sqrt{d}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}\rVert).

Next by Assumption
2.3
, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\leq\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+C\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}\rVert/\sqrt{d}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\rVert\big)\mathrm{d}s.

Combining the above two bounds yields

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\leq C(\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}+1)\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\rVert\Big)\mathrm{d}s+C\Big(\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+\frac{\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+1\Big).

(106)

Hence by Gronwall’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\leq C\exp(C(\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}+1))\Big(\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+\frac{\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+1\Big).

Under Assumption
2.1
, we have almost surely that

\displaystyle\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\max\Big(\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert,\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}\rVert,\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\Big)\leq C,

(107)

so the conclusion follows from the first claim of Lemma
4.7
. ∎

Proof of Lemma
4.6
.

Here and throughout,
C>0
denotes a constant depending on
T
but not on

\gamma
, and changing from instance to instance.
We restrict to the almost-sure event where
Lemmas
4.7
and
4.8
hold,
and (
107
) holds for all large
n,d
.
Then, coupling (
4
) and (
105
)
by the same Brownian motion, for any
0\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big(\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}(\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{s}_{\gamma})\rVert+\lVert s(\bm{\theta}^{s};\widehat{\alpha}^{s})-s(\bm{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma};\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{s}_{\gamma})\rVert\Big)\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\Big[\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s}+s(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\Big]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sqrt{2}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert.

Applying Lipschitz continuity of
s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.2
and the bounds of
Lemma
4.8
and (
107
), this shows

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{s}_{\gamma}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{s}_{\gamma}\rVert\big)\mathrm{d}s+C\gamma\sqrt{d}+C\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert.

(108)

Similarly, using Assumption
2.3
and

W_{2}^{2}(d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{u_{j}},d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{v_{j}})\leq d^{-1}\lVert\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{v}\rVert^{2}
for
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\bigg\lVert\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{s},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta^{s}_{j}}\Big)-\mathcal{G}\Big(\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{s}_{\gamma},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\bar{\theta}^{s}_{\gamma,j}}\Big)\bigg\rVert\mathrm{d}s+\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\bigg\lVert\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{s},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta^{s}_{j}}\Big)\bigg\rVert\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert\Big)\mathrm{d}s+C\gamma.

Combining the above display with (
108
), we have
by Gronwall’s lemma

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert\leq C\gamma+\frac{C}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert.

(109)

By Lemma
4.7
, there exists some
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\limsup_{d\to\infty}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\mathbf{b}^{t}-\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\gamma\cdot\max(\log(1/\gamma),1)}.

Substituting this bound in (
109
)
proves the claim on
\alpha
. Noting that

\displaystyle W_{2}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta^{t_{1}}_{j},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}}_{j})},\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{(\theta_{j}^{*},\bar{\theta}^{t_{1}}_{\gamma,j},\ldots,\bar{\theta}_{\gamma,j}^{t_{m}})}\Big)\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\big(\theta^{t_{\ell}}_{j}-\bar{\theta}^{t_{\ell}}_{\gamma,j}\big)^{2}}\leq\sqrt{m}\cdot\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}_{\gamma}\rVert,

this proves also the claim on
\theta
, and the claim on
\eta
follows from

\|\bm{\eta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\eta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|\leq\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|
and the same argument.
∎

4.4
Completing the proof

Proof of Theorem
2.5
.

For part (a), by the triangle inequality,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\alpha^{t}\|\leq\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}\|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}-\alpha^{t}\|.

Since
\{\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
and
\{\bar{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}\}
are
piecewise constant with values equal to those of the discrete processes of
Section
4.1
,
Lemma
4.1
implies that the middle
term converges to 0 a.s. as
n,d\to\infty
. Then,
taking
n,d\rightarrow\infty
followed by
\gamma\rightarrow 0
and
applying also Lemmas
4.5

and
4.6

to bound the first and third terms in this limit, this shows (a).

For part (b), similarly combining Lemmas
4.1
,

4.5
, and
4.6

shows that almost surely, for any
m\geq 1

and
t_{0},t_{1},\ldots,t_{m}\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\big(\theta_{j}^{*},\theta^{t_{0}}_{j},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}}_{j}\big)}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t_{0}},\ldots,\theta^{t_{m}}).

(110)

We now strengthen this to almost-sure convergence in the Wasserstein-2 sense
over
\mathbb{R}\times C([0,T])
, equipped with the product norm

\|\theta\|_{\infty}:=|\theta^{*}|+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\theta^{t}|.

By
[
54
, Definition 6.8 and Theorem 6.9]
, it suffices to show weak convergence together with convergence
of the squared norm
\|\theta\|_{\infty}^{2}
, which will be
implied by convergence for all pseudo-Lipschitz test functions
f:\mathbb{R}\times C([0,T])\to\mathbb{R}
satisfying

\big|f(\theta)-f(\theta^{\prime})\big|\leq C\|\theta-\theta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}(1+\|\theta\|_{\infty}+\|\theta^{\prime}\|_{\infty}).

(111)

Consider the event
\mathcal{E}
where
(
110
) holds for each
m\geq 2
and

\{t_{0},t_{1},t_{2},\ldots,t_{m}\}=\{0,\gamma,2\gamma,\ldots,T\}
with
\gamma=T/m
.
Let
\{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a piecewise linear interpolation
of
\{\bm{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]\cap\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, and similarly let

\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
be a piecewise linear interpolation of the
DMFT process
\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
. For
any pseudo-Lipschitz function
f:\mathbb{R}\times C([0,T])\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\Big|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\theta_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})-\mathbb{E}[f(\theta^{*},\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})]\Big|\leq(I)+(II)+(III)

(112)

with

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\tilde{\theta}_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})-\mathbb{E}[f(\theta^{*},\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})]\Big|

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle=\Big|\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\theta_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})-f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\tilde{\theta}_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})\Big|

\displaystyle(III)

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}[f(\theta^{*},\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})]-\mathbb{E}[f(\theta^{*},\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})]\Big|.

For a piecewise linear process
\tilde{\theta}
with knots at
\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\tilde{\theta}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})
may be understood as a function
of

(\theta_{j}^{*},\tilde{\theta}^{0},\tilde{\theta}^{\gamma},\tilde{\theta}^{2\gamma},\ldots,\tilde{\theta}^{T})
,
where this function is pseudo-Lipschitz on
\mathbb{R}^{m+2}

by the pseudo-Lipschitz property (
111
) for
f
. Then
on the above event
\mathcal{E}
, the
Wasserstein-2 convergence (
110
) implies

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}(I)=0.

To bound
(II)
, let
C,C^{\prime}>0
be constants depending on
T
(but not
\gamma
)
and changing from instance to instance.
Writing
\theta_{j}=(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\theta_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})\in\mathbb{R}\times C([0,T],\mathbb{R})

and applying (
111
), we have

(II)\leq\frac{C}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}-\tilde{\theta}_{j}\|_{\infty}\big(1+\|\theta_{j}\|_{\infty}\big)\leq C^{\prime}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}-\tilde{\theta}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(1+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

(113)

Set

F(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})={-}\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{y})+s(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})

so that by definition,

\theta_{j}^{t}=\theta_{j}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}F(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,b_{j}^{t}
. Hence

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\theta_{j}^{t})^{2}\leq C\Big((\theta_{j}^{0})^{2}+\int_{0}^{T}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}F(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s}))^{2}\mathrm{d}s+\|b_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big),

so

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}(\theta_{j}^{*})^{2}+(\theta_{j}^{0})^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|F(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|b_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big).

On an almost-sure event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, for all large
n,d
, we have that

d^{-1}\sum_{j}(\theta_{j}^{*})^{2}+d^{-1}\sum_{j}(\theta_{j}^{0})^{2}\leq C
by
Assumption
2.2
, that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\|F(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|^{2}\leq C
by the
definition of
F(\cdot)
together with Assumption
2.2

and Lemma
4.8
,
and that
d^{-1}\sum_{j}\|b_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C
by Doob’s maximal inequality

\mathbb{P}[\|b_{j}\|_{\infty}>x]\leq 2e^{-x^{2}/(2T)}
and Bernstein’s inequality for a sum
of independent subexponential random variables
[
61
, Theorem 2.8.1]
.
Thus, on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}.

(114)

Now fixing any
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
, define the Hölder semi-norm

\|\theta_{j}\|_{\alpha}=\sup_{s,t\in[0,T]}|\theta_{j}^{t}-\theta_{j}^{s}|/|t-s|^{\alpha}
.
Then, since
\tilde{\theta}_{j}
linearly interpolates
\theta_{j}
at the knots

\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\|\theta_{j}-\tilde{\theta}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\gamma^{\alpha}\|\theta_{j}\|_{\alpha}.

We have by definition
\theta_{j}^{t}-\theta_{j}^{s}=\int_{s}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}F(\bm{\theta}^{r},\widehat{\alpha}^{r})\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{2}(b_{j}^{t}-b_{j}^{s})
, so
by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle|\theta_{j}^{t}-\theta_{j}^{s}|

\displaystyle\leq|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}\big|\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}F(\bm{\theta}^{r},\widehat{\alpha}^{r})\big|^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1-\alpha}+\sqrt{2}|b_{j}^{t}-b_{j}^{s}|

\displaystyle\leq C|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(\Big(\int_{0}^{T}(\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}F(\bm{\theta}^{r},\widehat{\alpha}^{r}))^{2}\Big)^{1/2}+\|b_{j}\|_{\alpha}\Big)

and hence

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|F(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|^{2}+\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|b_{j}\|_{\alpha}^{2}\Big).

On an almost-sure event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
, for all large
n,d
, we have

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}d^{-1}\|F(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|^{2}\leq C
as
above, and
d^{-1}\sum_{j}\|b_{j}\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C
by the tail bound

\mathbb{P}[\|b_{j}\|_{\alpha}>C+x]\leq e^{-cx^{2}}
for some
C,c>0
(see e.g.
[
62
, Theorem 5.32, Example 5.37]
)
and Bernstein’s inequality. Thus on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
,

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\|\theta_{j}-\tilde{\theta}_{j}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C\gamma^{2\alpha}.

(115)

Applying (
114
) and (
115
) to
(
113
), on
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
,

\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}(II)<C\gamma^{\alpha}.

To bound
(III)
, similarly we have

(III)\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\Big(1+\mathbb{E}\|\theta\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}.

(116)

By definition

\theta^{t}=\theta^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big[{-}\delta\beta(\theta^{s}-\theta^{*})+s(\theta^{s},\alpha^{s})+\int_{0}^{s}R_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})(\theta^{s^{\prime}}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}s^{\prime}+u^{s}\Big]\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}b^{t}.

Hence, applying
|s(\theta^{s},\alpha^{s})|\leq C(1+|\theta^{s}|+\|\alpha^{s}\|)
by
Assumption
2.2
and uniform boundedness of the continuous
functions
\alpha^{s}
and
R_{\eta}(s,s^{\prime})
over
[0,T]
,

(\theta^{t})^{2}\leq C\Big(1+(\theta^{0})^{2}+(\theta^{*})^{2}+\|u\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\sup_{r\in[0,s]}(\theta^{s})^{2}\Big)\mathrm{d}s\Big).

Then Gronwall’s lemma gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(\theta^{t})^{2}\leq C\Big(1+(\theta^{0})^{2}+(\theta^{*})^{2}+\|u\|_{\infty}^{2}+\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}\Big).

We have
\mathbb{E}(\theta^{0})^{2},\mathbb{E}(\theta^{*})^{2}\leq C
by assumption.
Since
\{u^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
has covariance
C_{\eta}(t,s)
satisfying

|C_{\eta}(t,s)|\leq C|t-s|
by the condition (
32
) defining

\mathcal{S}(T)^{\text{cont}}
, we have
\mathbb{P}[\|u\|_{\infty}\geq C+t]\leq e^{-ct^{2}}

for some constants
C,c>0
by a standard application of Dudley’s inequality

[
61
, Theorem 8.1.6]
, so
\mathbb{E}\|u\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C
.
Similarly
\mathbb{E}\|b\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C
, so this gives

\mathbb{E}\|\theta\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C.

(117)

By definition we have also

\theta^{t}-\theta^{s}=\int_{s}^{t}\Big[{-}\delta\beta(\theta^{r}-\theta^{*})+s(\theta^{r},\alpha^{r})+\int_{0}^{r}R_{\eta}(r,r^{\prime})(\theta^{r^{\prime}}-\theta^{*})\mathrm{d}r^{\prime}+u^{r}\Big]\mathrm{d}r+\sqrt{2}(b^{t}-b^{s}),

so

\displaystyle|\theta^{t}-\theta^{s}|

\displaystyle\leq C|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}\Big(1+|\theta^{*}|+\sup_{r^{\prime}\in[0,r]}|\theta^{r^{\prime}}|+|u^{r}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1-\alpha}+\sqrt{2}|b^{t}-b^{s}|

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(1+\|\theta\|_{\infty}+\|u\|_{\infty}+\|b\|_{\alpha}\Big).

Then

\mathbb{E}\|\theta\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C(1+\mathbb{E}\|\theta\|_{\infty}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|u\|_{\infty}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|b\|_{\alpha}^{2})\leq C^{\prime},

so

\mathbb{E}\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq\gamma^{2\alpha}\mathbb{E}\|\theta\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C^{\prime}\gamma^{2\alpha}.

(118)

Applying (
117
) and (
118
) to
(
116
) shows

(III)\leq C\gamma^{\alpha}.

Applying these bounds for
(I)
,
(II)
, and
(III)
to take the limit
n,d\to\infty
followed by
\gamma\to 0
in (
112
), this shows that on
the almost-sure event
\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
(which does not depend
on
f
), for every pseudo-Lipschitz function
f:\mathbb{R}\times C([0,T])\to\mathbb{R}
,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}f(\theta_{j}^{*},\{\theta_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})=\mathbb{E}[f(\theta^{*},\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})].

This implies on
\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
that

\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta_{j}^{*},\{\theta_{j}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(\theta^{*},\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}).

For the convergence for
\bm{\eta}
, we note that

\eta_{i}^{t}=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t}+\int_{0}^{t}\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}F(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{b}^{t}
. Then applying
similar arguments as above, fixing any
\alpha\in(0,1/2)
,
on an almost-sure event we have for all large
n,d
that

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\eta_{i}\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C,\qquad\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\|\eta_{i}\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C\gamma^{2\alpha}.

For the DMFT process we have
\eta^{t}={-}\beta\int_{0}^{t}R_{\theta}(t,s)(\eta^{s}+w^{*}-\varepsilon)\mathrm{d}s-w^{t}
, hence

(\eta^{t})^{2}\leq C\Big((w^{*})^{2}+(w^{t})^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}+\int_{0}^{t}(\eta^{s})^{2}\mathrm{d}s\Big)

so Gronwall’s lemma and a similar argument as above
gives
\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq C
. Also

|\eta^{t}-\eta^{s}|\leq C|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(\int_{s}^{t}\Big(|w^{*}|+|\varepsilon|+|\eta^{r}|\Big)^{\frac{1}{1-\alpha}}\mathrm{d}r\Big)^{1-\alpha}+|w^{t}-w^{s}|\leq C^{\prime}|t-s|^{\alpha}\Big(|w^{*}|+|\varepsilon|+\|\eta\|_{\infty}+\|w\|_{\alpha}\Big)

so

\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C\Big(\mathbb{E}(w^{*})^{2}+\mathbb{E}\varepsilon^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{\infty}^{2}+\mathbb{E}\|w\|_{\alpha}^{2}\Big).

We recall that
\{w^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
has covariance satisfying

|C_{\theta}(t,s)|\leq C|t-s|
, so
\mathbb{P}[\|w\|_{\alpha}>C+x]\leq 2e^{-cx^{2}}

for some
C,c>0
(c.f.
[
62
, Theorem 5.32]
).
Thus
\mathbb{E}\|\eta\|_{\alpha}^{2}\leq C
. Applying these bounds,
the same arguments as above show the almost-sure convergence

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\delta_{\eta_{i}^{*},\varepsilon_{i},\{\eta_{i}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})}\overset{W_{2}}{\to}\mathsf{P}(\eta^{*},\varepsilon,\{\eta^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]})

where we recall that
\eta^{*}
on the right side is, by definition,

\eta^{*}={-}w^{*}
.
∎

5
Convergence of the linear response

In this section, we prove Theorem
2.8
. We assume throughout Assumptions
2.1
,
2.2
,
2.3
and the Hölder-continuity conditions of Theorem
2.8
.
We first state and prove in Section
5.1
an
analogue of Theorem
2.8
for the discrete-time dynamics
introduced previously in Section
4.1
, and
then analyze the discretization error and complete the proof of
Theorem
2.8
in Section

5.2
.

5.1
Convergence of response functions for discrete dynamics

We recall the discrete, integer-indexed dynamics
(
55
–
56
), which we reproduce
here as

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}-\gamma\Big[\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}-\mathbf{y})-s(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma})\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t}),\quad\bm{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t}

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma})\Big),\quad\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\delta_{\theta_{j}}.

(119)

We first show an analogue of Theorem
2.8
for these discrete
dynamics.

For any
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and any
j\in[d]
or
i\in[n]
, letting

\mathbf{e}_{j}
denote the
j^{\text{th}}
standard basis vector in either
\mathbb{R}^{d}
or

\mathbb{R}^{n}
, define two sets of perturbed dynamics

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}-\gamma\Big[\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y})-s(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\mathbf{e}_{j}\bm{1}_{t=s}\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t})

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}_{\gamma})\Big),

(120)

and

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t+1,[s,i],\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}-\gamma\Big[\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y})-s(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon})-\varepsilon\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}\bm{1}_{t=s}\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t})

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t+1,[s,i],\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}_{\gamma})\Big)

(121)

with the same initial conditions as (
119
). We set

\displaystyle\bm{\eta}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}=\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}.

(122)

Comparing with (
119
), these
dynamics have a perturbation to the drift in the direction of
\mathbf{e}_{j}
or

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}
at the single time
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
.
Let
\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=(\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s))_{i,j=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=(\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s))_{i,j=1}^{n}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be matrices of response functions defined by

\displaystyle(\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s))_{i,j}=\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\theta_{\gamma,i}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rangle,\qquad(\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s))_{i,j}=\delta\beta^{2}\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\eta_{\gamma,i}^{t,[s,j],\varepsilon}\rangle,

where
\langle\cdot\rangle
denotes the expectation over only the randomness
of
\{\mathbf{b}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
,
i.e. conditional on
(\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon})
and on the initial conditions

(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{0,(s,j),\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{0,(s,j),\varepsilon})=(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{0,[s,i],\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{0,[s,i],\varepsilon})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
.

Recall also the discrete-time DMFT response functions

R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
defined by
(
60
) and (
64
).
The goal of this section is to prove the following analogue of the
convergence statements for the response functions in Theorem
2.8
.

Lemma 5.1
.

For any fixed
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
s<t
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),\qquad\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s).

To ease notation, in the remainder of this section
we will drop all subscripts
\gamma
and write simply

\bm{\theta}^{t}=\bm{\theta}^{t}_{\gamma}
,
\widehat{\alpha}^{t}=\widehat{\alpha}^{t}_{\gamma}
,

\mathbf{b}^{t}=\mathbf{b}^{t}_{\gamma}
etc. to refer to the above discrete-time processes.
We first establish in Section
5.1.1
a set of dynamical cavity
estimates, which we will then use to prove Lemma

5.1
in Section

5.1.2
.

5.1.1
Dynamical cavity estimates

We introduce the following notations: For any
j\in[d]
and
i\in[n]
, denote

\bm{\theta}^{t}=(\theta^{t}_{j},\bm{\theta}^{t}_{-j})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad\theta^{t}_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\bm{\theta}^{t}_{-j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1},

\bm{\eta}^{t}=(\eta^{t}_{i},\bm{\eta}^{t}_{-i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad\eta^{t}_{i}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\bm{\eta}^{t}_{-i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1},

where
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\bm{\eta}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
are the components of the
discrete-time process (
119
), and

\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t}
are the coordinates of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
excluding the
j^{\text{th}}

(and similarly for
\bm{\eta}^{t}
).

We consider the following leave-one-out versions of
(
119
): For
j\in[d]
, let

\mathbf{X}^{(j)}=(X_{ik}\mathbf{1}_{k\neq j})_{i,k}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d},\qquad\mathbf{y}^{(j)}=\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{*}+\bm{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

(123)

where
\mathbf{X}^{(j)}
denotes
\mathbf{X}
with
j^{\text{th}}
column set to 0. Define

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t+1,(j)}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}-\gamma\Big[\beta(\mathbf{X}^{(j)})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}-\mathbf{y}^{(j)})-s(\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,(j)}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)})\Big)

(124)

with initialization
(\bm{\theta}^{0,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{0,(j)})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
, and write as above

\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}=(\theta^{t,(j)}_{j},\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}_{-j})\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\qquad\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}_{-j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}.

We note that for convenience of the proof, we define
\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}
to be of
the same dimension as
\bm{\theta}
, where one may check from (
124
) that the dynamics of
\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}_{-j}
do not involve
\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}
.
Similarly, for
i\in[n]
, let

\mathbf{X}^{[i]}=(X_{kj}\mathbf{1}_{k\neq i})_{k,j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d},\qquad\mathbf{y}^{[i]}=\mathbf{X}^{[i]}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}+\bm{\varepsilon},

where
\mathbf{X}^{[i]}
sets the
i^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{X}
to 0. Define

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t+1,[i]}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}-\gamma\Big[\beta(\mathbf{X}^{[i]})^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{[i]}\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}-\mathbf{y}^{[i]})-s(\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]})\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,[i]}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]})\Big)

(125)

also with initialization

(\bm{\theta}^{0,[i]},\widehat{\alpha}^{0,[i]})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
, and write as above

\bm{\eta}^{t,[i]}=(\eta^{t,[i]}_{i},\bm{\eta}^{t,[i]}_{-i})\in\mathbb{R}^{n},\qquad\eta^{t,[i]}_{i}\in\mathbb{R},\qquad\bm{\eta}^{t,[i]}_{-i}\in\mathbb{R}^{n-1}.

By construction,
\{\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\}

is independent of the
j^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathbf{X}
, and
\{\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\}
is independent of the
i^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{X}
.

The following lemma gives
\ell_{2}
estimates on the original
dynamics (
119
) as well as on its
difference with the cavity versions
(
124
) and (
125
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Fix any
T>0
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
(depending on
T
but not

\gamma
) such that for any
\gamma>0
, almost surely for all large
n,d
,
we have
for all
0\leq t\leq T/\gamma
and all
j\in[d]
,
i\in[n]
that

\displaystyle\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\|\leq C,\quad\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\rVert\leq C,\quad\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\rVert\leq C,

(126)

\displaystyle|\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}|\leq C(1+|\theta^{0}_{j}|+\max_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}|b^{t}_{j}|),

(127)

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}-\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\leq C(|\theta^{0}_{j}|+|\theta^{\ast}_{j}|+\max_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}|b^{t}_{j}|+\sqrt{\log d}),

(128)

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}-\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\leq C(|\varepsilon_{i}|+\sqrt{\log d}).

(129)

Proof.

Fixing a constant
C_{0}>0
large enough (depending on
T
)
and any
\gamma>0
, define the event

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle=\Big\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\,\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|\leq C_{0},\,\|\bm{\theta}^{*}\|_{2},\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|_{2}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d},\,\|\bm{\varepsilon}\|_{2}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d},\,

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\max_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d}\text{ for all large }n,d\Big\}.

Note that we have
\mathbf{b}^{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,t\gamma\mathbf{I})
,
so
\mathbb{P}[\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|_{2}>C_{0}\sqrt{t\gamma d}]\leq e^{-cd}

for some constants
C_{0},c>0
and all large
n,d

by a chi-squared tail bound. Then, taking a union
bound over all
t\in[0,T/\gamma]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and applying the
conditions of Assumption
2.1
together with the Borel-Cantelli
lemma, we see that this event
\mathcal{E}
holds almost surely.

We restrict to the event
\mathcal{E}
.
Let
C,C^{\prime}>0
denote constants depending
on
C_{0},T
(but not on
\gamma
) and changing from instance to instance.
For (
126
), we have by definition of

\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}
in
(
119
) that

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{0}{-}\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Big[\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{s}-\mathbf{y})-s(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})\Big]+\sqrt{2}\mathbf{b}^{t}

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{0}+\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\mathcal{G}\Big(\widehat{\alpha}^{s},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{s})\Big).

Applying the bounds for
s(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)

in Assumptions
2.2
and
2.3
and the conditions
of
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Big(\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|+\sqrt{d}\,\|\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\|+\sqrt{d}\Big)+\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|+\sqrt{2}\,\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq C\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Big(\|\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\|+\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|/\sqrt{d}+1\Big)+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|

so

1+\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\|\leq C\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\Big(1+\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{s}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\|\Big)+1+\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}\|\mathbf{b}^{t}\|.

Iterating this bound over
t
shows

\displaystyle\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{t}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)^{t}\bigg[1+\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|+\sqrt{\frac{2}{d}}\max_{s\in[0,t]}\|\mathbf{b}^{s}\|\bigg]\leq C^{\prime},

the last bound holding for
t\leq T/\gamma
and on
\mathcal{E}
. This establishes
the first claim of (
126
). The other two claims of
(
126
) for the cavity dynamics hold by the same argument, noting
that on
\mathcal{E}
we have also

\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\|\mathbf{X}^{[i]}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
for all

j\in[d]
and
i\in[n]
.

For (
127
), we have by definition of
(
124
) that

\theta^{t+1,(j)}_{j}=\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}+\gamma\,s(\theta^{t,(j)}_{j},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})+\sqrt{2}(b^{t+1}_{j}-b^{t}_{j}).

Then

\theta_{j}^{t,(j)}=\theta_{j}^{0}+\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}s(\theta_{j}^{s,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})+\sqrt{2}\,b_{j}^{t}

so applying the bound for
s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2

and the bound
\|\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\|\leq C
already shown in
(
126
),

1+|\theta_{j}^{t,(j)}|\leq C\gamma\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}(1+|\theta_{j}^{s,(j)}|)+1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+\sqrt{2}\,|b^{t}_{j}|.

Iterating this bound gives, for all
t\leq T/\gamma
,

1+|\theta_{j}^{t,(j)}|\leq(1+C\gamma)^{t}(1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+\sqrt{2}\max_{s\in[0,t]}|b_{j}^{t}|)\leq C^{\prime}(1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+\max_{t\leq T/\gamma}|b_{j}^{t}|)

which shows (
127
).

For (
128
), by definition,

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t+1}-\bm{\theta}^{t+1,(j)}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\Big)\bm{\theta}^{t}-\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\Big)\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}+\gamma\beta\Big(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\Big(s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})-s(\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})\Big).

Then, applying the Lipschitz bound for
s(\cdot)
in Assumption

2.2
and the conditions defining
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t+1}-\bm{\theta}^{t+1,(j)}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert+C\gamma\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t-1}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t-1,(j)}\rVert

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+C\gamma\underbrace{\big(\lVert(\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert+\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}\rVert\big)}_{:=\Delta_{t,j}}.

(130)

Similarly, by the Lipschitz bound for
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
in Assumption

2.3
,

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,(j)}\rVert\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\rVert+C\gamma\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert/\sqrt{d}.

Combining the above two inequalities yields

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t+1}-\bm{\theta}^{t+1,(j)}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,(j)}\rVert\leq(1+C\gamma)\big(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\rVert\big)+C\gamma\Delta_{t,j},

(131)

and hence iterating this bound and using

(\bm{\theta}^{0,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{0,(j)})=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
,
for any
t\leq T/\gamma
,

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\rVert\leq\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}(1+C\gamma)^{s}\max_{s=0}^{t-1}C\gamma\Delta_{s,j}\leq C^{\prime}\max_{s=0}^{t-1}\Delta_{s,j}.

Let us now bound
\Delta_{t,j}
. Writing
\mathbf{x}_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
for the

j^{\text{th}}
column of
\mathbf{X}
, we have

\mathbf{X}^{(j)}=\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{x}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}
, hence

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}=\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}+\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{e}_{j}\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}
, and

\displaystyle\lVert(\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert|\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|+\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}|\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}|\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|.

(132)

Similarly, we have

\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}=(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)})\bm{\theta}^{\ast}+(\mathbf{X}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)})^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}
so

\displaystyle\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{y}^{(j)}\rVert\leq C\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}|\theta^{\ast}_{j}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}|.

(133)

By (
127
), we have

|\theta^{t,(j)}_{j}|\leq C(1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+\sup_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}|b^{t}_{j}|)
for
t\leq\left\lfloor T/\gamma\right\rfloor
. Applying this in the above two bounds yields, on
\mathcal{E}
,

\displaystyle\Delta_{t,j}\leq C\Big[1+|\theta^{0}_{j}|+|\theta^{\ast}_{j}|+\sup_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}|b_{j}^{t}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}|\Big].

Define the additional event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
where

\sup_{j\in[d]}\max_{t\in[0,T/\gamma]}|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|+|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}|\leq C_{0}\sqrt{\log d}\text{ for all large }n,d.

Then the desired bound (
128
) holds for all large
n,d

on the event
\mathcal{E}\cap\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
,
so it remains to show that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
holds almost surely for sufficiently large
C_{0}>0
.
For each
j\in[d]
and
t\in[0,T/\gamma]
,
by independence between
\mathbf{x}_{j}
and

\mathbf{X}^{(j)},\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}
, we have that
\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}
is subgaussian conditional on

\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}
, so

\mathbb{P}\bigg[|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|\geq C\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\|\log d}{d}}\bigg]\leq e^{-cd}

for some constants
C,c>0
(conditional on
\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}
, and
hence also unconditionally). Then, taking a union bound over

j\in[d]
and
t\in[0,T/\gamma]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and applying
the Borel-Cantelli lemma, almost surely for all large
n,d
,

\sup_{j,t}|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|\leq C\sup_{j,t}\sqrt{\frac{\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\|\log d}{d}}.

On the event
\mathcal{E}
we have
\sup_{j,t}\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}
by (
126
) already shown, so

\sup_{j,t}|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)}|\leq C^{\prime}\sqrt{\log d}

a.s. for all large
n,d
. The terms

|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}|
and
|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\varepsilon}|

are bounded similarly, verifying that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
holds almost surely
as claimed, and concluding the proof of (
128
).

For (
129
), similar to above, we have

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t+1}-\bm{\theta}^{t+1,[i]}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}\rVert+C\gamma\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\rVert

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+C\gamma\underbrace{\Big(\lVert\mathbf{X}^{[i]\top}\mathbf{X}^{[i]}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X})\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}\rVert+\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{[i]\top}\mathbf{y}^{[i]}\rVert\Big)}_{\Delta_{t,i}},

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,[i]}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\rVert+C\gamma\big(\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\rVert+\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}\rVert/\sqrt{d}\big).

which implies

\displaystyle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\,\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}\rVert\leq C\max_{s=0}^{t-1}\Delta_{s,i}.

Using
\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{X}^{[i]}+\mathbf{e}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}
, where

\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
now denotes (the transpose of) the
i^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{X}
, we
have
\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}=\mathbf{X}^{[i]\top}\mathbf{X}^{[i]}+\mathbf{x}_{i}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}
and
\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{X}^{[i]\top}\mathbf{y}^{[i]}=\mathbf{x}_{i}(\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{\ast}+\varepsilon_{i})
, so

\displaystyle\Delta_{t,i}\leq\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Big(|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]}|+|\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\theta}^{*}|+|\varepsilon_{i}|\Big).

Using independence between
\mathbf{x}_{i}
and
\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]},\bm{\theta}^{*}
, we obtain
as above that on an almost sure event
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
, for all large
n,d

we have
\Delta_{t,i}\leq C(|\varepsilon_{i}|+\sqrt{\log d})
for all
t\in[0,T/\gamma]\cap\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
i\in[n]
, showing (
129
).
∎

5.1.2
Proof of Lemma
5.1

Lemma 5.3
.

For any
T>0
, on the event where
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
, there exists a constant

C>0
(depending on
T,C_{0}
but not
\gamma
) such that

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T/\gamma}\max_{j\in[d]}\Big[\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rVert\Big]\leq C\gamma,

\displaystyle\max_{0\leq s\leq t\leq T/\gamma}\max_{i\in[n]}\Big[\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\rVert\Big]\leq C\gamma.

Proof.

For the first statement, we fix
s,j
, and shorthand

\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}
as
\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}
. By definition of the process
(
120
), we have for
t\geq s+1

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}))\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon},

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Big(1+\gamma\mathrm{d}_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t}))\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\theta}}\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t}))\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}.

Then applying the conditions for
s(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
in
Assumptions
2.2
and
2.3

and
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t+1,\varepsilon}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}\rVert+C\sqrt{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\rVert,

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,\varepsilon}\rVert

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\rVert+C\gamma\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}\rVert/\sqrt{d},

where
C>0
is a constant independent of
\gamma
. Combining and iterating these
inequalities yields, for all
t\in[s+1,T/\gamma]
,

\displaystyle\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t+1,\varepsilon}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,\varepsilon}\rVert\leq(1+C\gamma)^{t-s}\big(\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rVert\big)\leq C^{\prime}\gamma,

(134)

using
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=\gamma\mathbf{e}_{j}
and
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=0
. This holds for all
s\in[0,T/\gamma]

and
j\in[d]
, showing the first claim. The proof of the second claim is
analogous, and omitted for brevity.
∎

Lemma 5.4
.

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}
be given by (
119
). For each
t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
define the matrix

\displaystyle\bm{\Omega}^{t}=\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}.

(135)

Then for any fixed
s,t\in[0,T/\gamma]
with
t\geq s+1
,
almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}-\frac{\gamma}{d}\Tr\Big(\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\Big)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}-\frac{\gamma}{n}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle=0

where by convention we set
\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}=\mathbf{I}
for
t=s+1
.

Proof.

Let us denote

\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})=\Big(\nabla_{\alpha}s(\theta_{i}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})^{\top}\Big)_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K},\quad\mathbf{r}^{t,(s,j)}=\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=\underbrace{\Big(I-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}))\Big)}_{\bm{\Omega}^{t}}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}+\gamma\mathbf{r}^{t,(s,j)}.

Iterating this identity with
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=\gamma\mathbf{e}_{j}
shows

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\gamma\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\mathbf{e}_{j}+\gamma\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1}\mathbf{r}^{\ell,(s,j)}

(136)

(where
\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1}=\mathbf{I}
for
\ell=t-1
).
This implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}=\frac{\gamma}{d}\Tr\big(\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\big)+\frac{\gamma}{d}\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{t-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1}\mathbf{r}^{\ell,(s,j)}.

(137)

On an event where
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
for all large
n,d
(which holds almost
surely), by the Lipschitz continuity of
s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.1
and bound in Lemma
5.3
,
we have
\|\bm{\Omega}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C
,

\|\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d}
, and

\|\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\|\leq C/\sqrt{d}
for all
t
,
where
C>0
is a constant (possibly depending on
\gamma
) changing
from instance to instance. Then by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\mathbf{e}_{j}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1}\mathbf{r}^{\ell,(s,j)}\Big|\leq\sqrt{\Big\|\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1}\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{\ell},\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell})\Big\|_{F}^{2}}\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{d}\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rVert^{2}}\leq C\sqrt{d},

which implies that the second term of (
137
) converges to 0
a.s. as
n,d\to\infty
. This proves the first claim.
The proof of the second claim is analogous, and omitted for brevity.
∎

Let us now introduce a notation for the discrete DMFT response process
(
57
) prior to taking an expectation.
Fixing a univariate process
\theta=\{\theta^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and
\mathbb{R}^{K}
-valued
process
\alpha=\{\alpha^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
as inputs, define the following
auxiliary process
\{r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)\}_{s<t}
:

\displaystyle r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t+1,s)

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}\gamma&\text{ for }s=t,\\
\Big(1-\gamma\delta\beta+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta^{t},\alpha^{t})\Big)r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)+\gamma\sum_{\ell=s+1}^{t-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,\ell)r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(\ell,s)&\text{ for }s<t.\end{cases}

Note that if the inputs
\{\theta^{t},\alpha^{t}\}
are given by the
discrete-time DMFT processes defined in (
57
) and
(
60
), then

\displaystyle r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)=\frac{\partial\theta_{\gamma}^{t}}{\partial u^{s}_{\gamma}},\qquad\mathbb{E}[r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)]=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)

which are precisely the auxiliary process defined in (
58
) and DMFT
response function in (
60
). We will instead consider
(
5.1.2
) with inputs
\{\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
given by the coordinates of
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}
solving
(
119
).

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
\{\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be defined by
(
119
), and let

R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
be the response function of its DMFT limit
defined in (
60
).
Then for any fixed
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
s<t
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s)=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s).

Proof.

First note that by the Lipschitz bound for
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)

in Assumption
2.2
, the a.s. convergence

\{\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\}\to\{\alpha^{t}\}
in Lemma
4.1
,
and a simple induction argument, we have almost surely

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\Big(\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s)-\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\alpha)}(t,s)\Big)=0.

By a similar induction argument using the boundedness and
Lipschitz-continuity of
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
, for each fixed
s<t
,
the map
(\theta_{j}^{0},\ldots,\theta_{j}^{t})\mapsto r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\alpha)}(t+1,s)
is Lipschitz for each
j
.
Then by the empirical Wasserstein-2 convergence for
\bm{\theta}^{0},\ldots,\bm{\theta}^{T}

in (
66
) of
Lemma
4.1
, almost surely

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\alpha)}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}[r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)]

where the inputs

(\theta,\alpha)
on the right side are the discrete-time DMFT processes
(
57
) and
(
60
), and
\mathbb{E}[\cdot]
is the expectation over their
law. The lemma follows from noting that, by
definition,
R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\mathbb{E}[r_{\theta}^{(\theta,\alpha)}(t,s)]
.
∎

We now proceed to prove Lemma
5.1
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

For any fixed
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
s<t
,
set also

r_{\eta}(t,s)=\frac{\partial\eta^{t}}{\partial w^{s}}=(\delta\beta)^{-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)

and define the error terms

\displaystyle E_{\theta}^{t,(s,j)}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}-r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s),

(139)

\displaystyle E_{\eta}^{t,[s,i]}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}-\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t,s).

(140)

We first prove by induction on
t
that for any
p\geq 1

and
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
s<t
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|E_{\theta}^{t,(s,j)}|^{p}=0,\quad\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|E_{\theta}^{t,[s,i]}|^{p}=0.

(141)

Fixing any
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
, the base case
t=s+1
holds, as
direct calculation via (
120
) shows that

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=\gamma=r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(s+1,s),\quad j\in[d],

and similarly via (
121
),

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{s+1,[s,i],\varepsilon}=\gamma\lVert\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}\rVert^{2}=\gamma+E_{\eta}^{s+1,[s,i]}

where
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|E_{\eta}^{s+1,[s,i]}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. under Assumption
2.1
while
r_{\eta}(s+1,s)=\beta R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(s+1,s)=\gamma\beta
.

Suppose by induction that (
141
) holds for this fixed
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
up to time
t
.
Note that Lemmas
5.4
and
5.5

then imply, with the matrix

\bm{\Omega}^{t}
defined in (
135
), for each
s<t
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\bigg\{\gamma\cdot\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\Big),\;\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),

(142)

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\bigg\{\gamma\cdot\frac{1}{n}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big),\;\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\bigg\}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t,s)=(\delta\beta^{2})^{-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s).

Claim for
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}
.

We establish the claim (
141
) for
E_{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j)}
. Fixing both
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
and
j\in[d]
, let us shorthand

\bm{\theta}^{t,(s,d),\varepsilon}
as
\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}

and recall the notations
\bm{\theta}^{t}=(\theta^{t}_{j},\bm{\theta}^{t}_{-j})
from Section

5.1.1
where
\theta_{j}^{t}
is the
j
th coordinate
of
\bm{\theta}^{t}
. Writing correspondingly
\mathbf{X}=(\mathbf{x}_{j},\mathbf{X}_{-j})
, we have

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\Big)\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}(\mathbf{x}_{j}\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y})+\gamma s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon})+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}_{-j}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}_{-j}^{t})

\displaystyle\theta_{j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\gamma\beta\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert^{2}\Big)\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y})+\gamma s(\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon})+\sqrt{2}(b_{j}^{t+1}-b_{j}^{t})

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}))

Define

\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})=\Big(\nabla_{\alpha}s(\theta_{i}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})^{\top}\Big)_{i=1}^{d}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times K},\quad\mathbf{r}^{t}=\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then, taking the derivative of
\theta_{j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}
in
\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\Big(1-\gamma\beta\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert^{2}+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma r_{j}^{t}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}.

(143)

Taking the derivative of
\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}
in
\varepsilon
,

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}=\underbrace{\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t-1},\widehat{\alpha}^{t-1}))\Big)}_{:=\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t-1,\varepsilon}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t-1,\varepsilon}+\gamma\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{t-1}.

Then, iterating this equality and using
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{s+1,(s,j),\varepsilon}=0
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=-\gamma\beta\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{k,\varepsilon}+\gamma\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,\varepsilon}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}.

Plugging the above expression into (
143
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Big(1-\gamma\beta\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert^{2}+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\Big)\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon}}_{(I_{j})}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+(\gamma\beta)^{2}\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\underbrace{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{k,\varepsilon}}_{(II_{j,k})}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\gamma^{2}\beta\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\underbrace{\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}}_{(III_{j,k})}+\underbrace{\gamma r_{j}^{t}}_{(IV_{j})}.

(144)

Analysis of
(I_{j})
.
We have

(I_{j})=(1-\gamma\delta\beta+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}))r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s)+\mathsf{r}_{1}^{(j)},

(145)

where

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{1}^{(j)}=\gamma\beta(\delta-\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert^{2})(\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon})+\Big(1-\gamma\delta\beta+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\Big)E^{t,(s,j)}_{\theta}.

For any
p\geq 1
,
by the induction hypothesis we have

d^{-1}\sum_{j}|E^{t,(s,j)}_{\theta}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. By the conditions for
\mathbf{X}
in Assumption
2.1
,
d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\delta-\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|^{2}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. By Lemma
5.3
,

\max_{j}|\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,\varepsilon}|\leq C
a.s. for all large
n,d
, while by Assumption
2.2
,
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
is also bounded.
Combining these bounds gives

d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}_{1}^{(j)}|^{p}\to 0

a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Analysis of
(II_{j,k})
.
Let

\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t,(j)}=\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}))

be the analogue of
\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t}
defined by the cavity dynamics

\{\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}\}
.
We first show that a.s. for all large
n,d
, we have for every
j\in[d]
that

\displaystyle|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,1}|

\displaystyle:=\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}-\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\Big(|\theta^{0}_{j}|+|\theta^{\ast}_{j}|+\max_{u\in[0,t]}|b^{u}_{j}|+\sqrt{\log d}\Big).

(146)

To see this, note that

\displaystyle|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,1}|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{t-1}\underbrace{\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}(\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell}-\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)})\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\Big|}_{:=T^{\ell}_{(j,k)}}.

Here

\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell}-\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)}=\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell},\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell})-\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell,(j)}))
.
Then we may bound

\displaystyle|T^{\ell}_{(j,k)}|

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{2}\cdot\lVert\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell},\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell})-\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell,(j)})\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\gamma\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{\infty}\cdot\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell-1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\ldots\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert_{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\cdot(\lVert\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)}-\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{\ell}\rVert_{2}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell,(j)}-\widehat{\alpha}^{\ell}\rVert_{2}).

(147)

Since
\mathbf{x}_{j}
is independent of
\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t,(j)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{-j}
, we have by a
subgaussian tail bound

\mathbb{P}\Big[\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\geq C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,\|\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\|_{2}\Big]\leq e^{-cd}

for each
i=1,\ldots,d
and some constants
C,c>0
. Then, taking a union bound
and applying the Borel-Cantelli lemma, almost surely for all large
n,d
,

\sup_{j,\ell}\|\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}\|_{\infty}\leq\sup_{j,\ell}C\sqrt{\frac{\log d}{d}}\,\|\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ldots\|\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\|\mathbf{X}_{-j}\|_{\mathrm{op}}.

The right side is bounded by
C^{\prime}\sqrt{(\log d)/d}
on an almost-sure event
where
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
holds for all large
n,d
. Then, applying this to (
147
) and applying also
Lemma
5.2
to bound the last term of (
147
), this shows (
146
).
By the conditions of Assumption
2.1
and
the tail estimates of the Brownian motion in Lemma

4.7
, this bound (
146
) in turn implies

d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}_{2,1}^{(j,k)}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Now consider

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,2}:=\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{j}-\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\Big).

Since
\mathbf{x}_{j}
is independent of
\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t,(j)}
and
\mathbf{X}_{-j}
, the
Hanson-Wright inequality yields

\mathbb{P}\Big[|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,2}|\geq\max\Big(\frac{C\sqrt{\log d}}{d}\,\|\mathbf{W}\|_{F},\frac{C\log d}{d}\,\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\Big)\Big]\leq e^{-cd}

for some
C,c>0
, where

\mathbf{W}=\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}
.
Again taking a union bound over
j\in[d]
and applying
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}

and
\|\mathbf{W}\|_{F}\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d}
a.s. for all large
n,d
, this implies

d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,2}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Finally, let
\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
be the embedding of
\mathbf{X}_{-j}
with

j^{\text{th}}
column set to 0 as defined in (
123
), and let

\bm{\Omega}^{t,(j)}=\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)}))\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}.

Consider

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}_{2,3}^{(j,k)}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}_{-j}^{\top}\Big)-\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big)
