\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\Big)-\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1,(j)}(\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X})^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\sum_{\ell={k+1}}^{t-1}\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big(\mathbf{X}^{(j)}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{\ell+1,(j)}(\bm{\Omega}^{\ell}-\bm{\Omega}^{\ell,(j)})\bm{\Omega}^{\ell-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\frac{1}{d}\Tr\Big((\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X})\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\Big)

Almost surely for all large
n,d
, for every
j\in[d]
we have

\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X}\|_{F}=\|\mathbf{x}_{j}\|\leq C
and

\displaystyle\|\bm{\Omega}^{t,(j)}-\bm{\Omega}^{t}\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\beta\|\mathbf{X}^{(j)\top}\mathbf{X}^{(j)}-\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\|_{F}+\gamma\|\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})-\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+|\theta_{j}^{*}|+\max_{u\in[0,t]}|b_{j}^{u}|+\sqrt{\log d}\Big),

the second inequality applying the Lipschitz continuity of
s(\cdot)
in
Assumption
2.2
and Lemma
5.2
. Then, applying

\Tr(A-B)C\leq\|A-B\|_{F}\|C\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\,\|A-B\|_{F}\|C\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, we obtain
a.s. for all large
n,d
that for every
j\in[d]
,

|r_{2,3}^{(j,k)}|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{d}}\Big(1+|\theta_{j}^{0}|+|\theta_{j}^{*}|+\max_{u\in[0,t]}|b_{j}^{u}|+\sqrt{\log d}\Big),

which implies as above that

d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}_{2,3}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Combining these bounds for

\mathsf{r}_{2,1}^{(j,k)},\mathsf{r}_{2,2}^{(j,k)},\mathsf{r}_{2,3}^{(j,k)}
,
the second statement of
(
142
) for
almost sure convergence of

d^{-1}\Tr(\mathbf{X}\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{X}^{\top})
,
the induction hypothesis for
approximation of
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{k,\varepsilon}
by
r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}
, and
the bound
|\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{k,\varepsilon}|\leq C
a.s. for all large
n,d
by Lemma
5.3
, we get that

(II_{j,k})=\frac{1}{\gamma\beta^{2}}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,k)r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(k,s)+\mathsf{r}_{2}^{(j,k)}

(148)

where
d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}_{2}^{(j,k)}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Analysis of
(III_{j,k})
.
We apply a similar leave-one-out argument as above. Let

\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{t,(j)}=\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{d-1}.

(Note that we replace only the first factor

\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{t,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,(j)})
by the cavity
dynamics, leaving the second factor unchanged.) Then

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}-\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k,(j)}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\sum_{\ell=k+1}^{t-1}\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell+1,(j)}(\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell}-\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell,(j)})\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{\ell-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}\Big|}_{:=u^{(j,k)}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\underbrace{\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}(\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}-\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k,(j)})\Big|}_{:=v^{(j,k)}}.

(149)

We note that a.s. for all large
n,d
, we have

\|\mathbf{r}^{k}\|\leq C\sqrt{d}\cdot C/\sqrt{d}\leq C^{\prime}

by the Lipschitz bound for
s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2

and Lemma
5.3
. Then, using
similar arguments as in the analysis of
\mathsf{r}_{2,1}^{(j,k)}
above,
we have
d^{-1}\sum_{j}|u^{(j,k)}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.
For the second term, we have

\displaystyle v^{(j,k)}

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{j+1,(j)}\Big(\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k},\widehat{\alpha}^{k})-\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)})\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{k,\varepsilon}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}\rVert\lVert\mathbf{X}_{-j}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\ldots\lVert\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}(\lVert\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k}-\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{k}-\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)}\rVert)\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{k,\varepsilon}\rVert,

which satisfies
d^{-1}\sum_{j}|v^{(j,k)}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for all
p\geq 1
by Lemmas
5.2
and
5.3
. Thus

\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k}=\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k,(j)}+\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}

where
d^{-1}\sum_{j}|\mathsf{r}^{(j,k)}|^{p}\to 0
a.s.
On the other hand, we have

\displaystyle\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\mathbf{r}_{-j}^{k,(j)}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)})\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{k,\varepsilon}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)})\rVert\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{k,\varepsilon}\rVert.

Since
\mathbf{X}_{-j},\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t,(j)},\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)}

are all independent of
\mathbf{x}_{j}
, a subgaussian tail bound and union bound shows,
a.s. for all large
n,d
, that for every
j\in[d]
,

\lVert\mathbf{x}_{j}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-j}\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{t-1,(j)}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-j}^{k+1,(j)}\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}_{-j}^{k,(j)},\widehat{\alpha}^{k,(j)})\rVert\leq C\sqrt{\log d}.

Since
\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{k,\varepsilon}\rVert\leq C/\sqrt{d}

by Lemma
5.3
, this shows

(III_{j,k})=\mathsf{r}_{3}^{(j,k)}

(150)

where
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|\mathsf{r}_{3}^{(j,k)}|^{p}=0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Analysis of
(IV_{j})
.

By Assumption
2.2
and Lemma
5.3
,

|(IV_{j})|\leq\gamma\lVert\nabla_{\alpha}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\rVert\lVert\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\rVert\leq C/\sqrt{d}
a.s. for all large
n,d
,
hence

(IV_{j})=\mathsf{r}_{4}^{(j)}

(151)

where
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|\mathsf{r}_{4}^{(j)}|^{p}=0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Applying (
145
), (
148
),
(
150
), and (
151
) back to
(
144
),

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\big(1-\gamma\delta\beta+\gamma\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\big)r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s)+\gamma\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,k)r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(k,s)+E_{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j)}

\displaystyle=r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t+1,s)+E_{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j)}

where
\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}|E_{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j)}|^{p}=0

a.s. for each
p\geq 1
, concluding the proof the inductive claim (
141
) for
E_{\theta}^{t+1,(s,j)}
.

Claim for
\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\eta}_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}
.

We now show the claim (
141
) for
E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}
. Again fixing
s\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

and
i\in[n]
, let us
shorthand
\bm{\eta}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}
and
\bm{\theta}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}
as

\bm{\eta}^{t,\varepsilon}
and
\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}
. Let us write

\bm{\eta}^{t}=(\eta_{i}^{t},\bm{\eta}_{-i}^{t})
as in Section
5.1.1
,
and write correspondingly

\mathbf{y}=(y_{i},\mathbf{y}_{-i})
,
\bm{\varepsilon}=(\varepsilon_{i},\bm{\varepsilon}_{-i})
, and

\mathbf{X}=[\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}]^{\top}
where
\mathbf{x}_{i}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
denotes now (the transpose of) the
i^{\text{th}}

row

of
\mathbf{X}
, and
\mathbf{X}_{-i}\in\mathbb{R}^{(n-1)\times d}
. Then

\displaystyle\bm{\theta}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\Big[{-}\beta\big(\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}_{-i}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y}_{-i})+\mathbf{x}_{i}(\eta_{i}^{t,\varepsilon}-y_{i})\big)+s(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon})\Big]+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})

\displaystyle\eta_{i}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\eta_{i}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\Big[{-}\beta\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\big(\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}_{-i}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}-\mathbf{y}_{-i})+\mathbf{x}_{i}(\eta_{i}^{t,\varepsilon}-y_{i})\big)+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}s(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon})\Big]+\sqrt{2}\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})

\displaystyle\widehat{\alpha}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\cdot\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon})).

Set

\mathbf{r}^{t}=\nabla_{\alpha}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\widehat{\alpha}^{t,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Then, taking the derivative of
\eta_{i}^{t+1,\varepsilon}
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t+1,\varepsilon}=\Big(1-\gamma\beta\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t,\varepsilon}+\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\Big({-}\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-i}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t}))\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}+\gamma\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{t}.

(152)

Taking derivative of
\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}
yields

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\Big(\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-i}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t-1},\widehat{\alpha}^{t-1}))\Big)}_{:=\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t-1,\varepsilon}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{x}_{i}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t-1,\varepsilon}+\gamma\mathbf{r}^{t-1}.

Iterating this equality and using

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{s+1,[s,i],\varepsilon}=\gamma\mathbf{x}_{i}
gives

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\bm{\theta}^{t,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\gamma\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\gamma\beta\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{k+1}\mathbf{x}_{i}\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{k,\varepsilon}+\gamma\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}^{k}.

Plugging the above expression into (
152
), we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t+1,\varepsilon}=\underbrace{\Big(1-\gamma\beta\lVert\mathbf{x}_{i}\rVert^{2}\Big)\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t,\varepsilon}}_{(I_{i})}+\gamma\underbrace{\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t}-\mathbf{I})\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1}\mathbf{x}_{i}}_{(II_{i})}

\displaystyle\quad-\gamma\beta\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\underbrace{\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t}-\mathbf{I})\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{k+1}\mathbf{x}_{i}\cdot\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{k,\varepsilon}}_{(III_{i,k})}+\gamma\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}\underbrace{\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}(\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t}-\mathbf{I})\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{k+1}\mathbf{r}^{k}}_{(IV)_{i,k}}+\underbrace{\gamma\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{r}^{t}}_{(V_{i})}.

(153)

The arguments to analyze these terms are similar to the above, and we will
omit some details.

Analysis of
(I_{i})
.
By the induction hypothesis and
concentration of
\|\mathbf{x}_{i}\|^{2}
around 1,

(I_{i})=(1-\gamma\beta)\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t,s)+\mathsf{r}_{1}^{[i]}

(154)

where
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}_{1}^{[i]}|^{p}\to 0
a.s. for any
p\geq 1
.

Analysis of
(II_{i})
.
Let
\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t,[i]}=\mathbf{I}-\gamma\beta\mathbf{X}_{-i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}_{-i}+\gamma\diag(\partial_{\theta}s(\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}))
with

\bm{\theta}^{t,[i]},\widehat{\alpha}^{t,[i]}
given by the cavity dynamics of
(
125
). Set

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{[i]}_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1,[i]}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1,[i]}\mathbf{x}_{i}

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{[i]}_{2,2}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{x}_{i}^{\top}\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1,[i]}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1,[i]}\mathbf{x}_{i}-\frac{1}{d}\Tr\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1,[i]}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1,[i]}

\displaystyle\mathsf{r}^{[i]}_{2,3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{d}\Tr\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{t-1,[i]}\ldots\bm{\Omega}_{-i}^{s+1}-\frac{1}{d}\Tr\bm{\Omega}^{t-1}\ldots\bm{\Omega}^{s+1,[i]}.

Then the same arguments above yield

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}^{[i]}_{2,j}|^{p}\to 0
for each
j=1,2,3
.
Applying the same arguments for
t
in place of
t-1
, and
the first statement of (
142
) for both
t

and
t-1
,

(II_{i})=\gamma^{-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)-\gamma^{-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)+\mathsf{r}_{2}^{[i]}

(155)

where
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}_{2}^{[i]}|^{p}\to 0
a.s.

Analysis of
(III_{i,k})
,
(IV_{i,k})
, and
(V_{i})
.

Similar arguments as above show

\displaystyle(III_{i,k})

\displaystyle=(\gamma\beta)^{-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,k)r_{\eta}(k,s)-(\gamma\beta)^{-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,k)r_{\eta}(k,s)+\mathsf{r}_{3}^{[i,k]}

(156)

\displaystyle(IV)_{i,k}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{4}^{[i,k]}

(157)

\displaystyle(V)_{i}

\displaystyle=\mathsf{r}_{5}^{[i]}

(158)

where
n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}_{3}^{[i,k]}|^{p}\to 0
,

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}_{4}^{[i,k]}|^{p}\to 0
, and

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|\mathsf{r}_{5}^{[i]}|^{p}\to 0
a.s.

Applying (
154
), (
155
),
(
156
), (
157
), and (
158
)
back to (
153
), for an error term

E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}
satisfying

n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}|E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}|^{p}\to 0
a.s., we have

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\eta_{i}^{t+1,\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=(1-\gamma\beta)\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t,s)+R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)-R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt-\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}(R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,k)-R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s))r_{\eta}(k,s)+E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}

\displaystyle=\Big[{-}\gamma r_{\eta}(t,s)+R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)-\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,k)r_{\eta}(k,s)\Big]

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\underbrace{\Big[\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t,s)-R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)+\sum_{k=s+1}^{t-1}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,k)r_{\eta}(k,s)\Big]}_{=0}+E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}

\displaystyle=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,s)-\sum_{k=s+1}^{t}R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+1,k)r_{\eta}(k,s)+E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}

\displaystyle=\beta^{-1}r_{\eta}(t+1,s)+E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}.

This shows the inductive claim (
141
) for
E_{\eta}^{t+1,[s,i]}
, and hence concludes the induction.

To conclude the proof, by boundedness of
\partial_{\theta}s(\cdot)
and the definition (
5.1.2
),

d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})}(t,s)
is bounded
by a constant. Furthermore, by the expansion (
137
) and its
following arguments (which hold also at non-zero
\varepsilon>0
),
on the event
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
, we have that

d^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{\varepsilon}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}
is also bounded by a
constant for all sufficiently small
\varepsilon\geq 0
.
Then, writing
\langle\cdot\rangle
for the expectation over only
the discrete Brownian motion
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
, we may apply the dominated
convergence theorem to the first statement of (
141
) to get, almost surely,

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rangle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\theta_{j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rangle

\displaystyle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{d}\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle r_{\theta}^{(\theta_{j},\alpha)}(t,s)\rangle=R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s),

the last equality holding by Lemma

5.5
. Similarly we may apply the dominated convergence
theorem to the second statement of (
141
) to get, almost surely,

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\delta\beta^{2}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\rangle=\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\delta\beta\cdot\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\langle r_{\eta}(t,s)\rangle=R_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s),

concluding the proof.
∎

5.2
Discretization of Langevin response function

In the following, we denote
\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
and consider (
4
–
5
) as a joint diffusion in the variables
\mathbf{x}^{t}=(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})
.
Let
u:\mathbb{R}^{d+K}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
and
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+K)\times(d+K)}
be defined by

\displaystyle u(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=u(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})=\Big({-}\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}-\mathbf{y})+\big(s(\theta_{j},\widehat{\alpha})\big)_{j=1}^{d},\;\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha},\widehat{\mathsf{P}}(\bm{\theta}))\Big)

(159)

\displaystyle\mathbf{M}

\displaystyle=\diag(\mathbf{I}_{d\times d},0_{K\times K})

Given an initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
, we consider the continuous-time
dynamics for
\mathbf{x}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
and
\mathbf{V}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+K)\times(d+K)}

defined by

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{x}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}u(\mathbf{x}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\mathbf{b}^{t}

\displaystyle\qquad\mathbf{V}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}_{d+K}+\int_{0}^{t}[\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{s})\mathbf{V}^{s}]\mathrm{d}s

(160)

where
\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{(d+K)\times(d+K)}
is the derivative of
u(\cdot)
at

\mathbf{x}
. We consider also a piecewise-constant version of these dynamics

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{x}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}u(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\mathbf{b}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{I}_{d+K}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}[\mathrm{d}u(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{s})\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{s}]\mathrm{d}s

(161)

where
\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor\in\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
is as previously defined in
(
92
). We note that the process

\mathbf{x}^{t}=(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})
in
(
160
) is precisely our adaptive Langevin process of interest
(
4
–
5
). Similarly, the process
\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t}
in (
161
) is the piecewise-constant embedding
from Section
4.3
of
the discrete dynamics for
\mathbf{x}_{\gamma}^{t}=(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{t})

which we have rewritten in (
119
). Denoting

[t]=\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor/\gamma\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
as in (
92
), we have

\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{x}_{\gamma}^{[t]}=(\bm{\theta}_{\gamma}^{[t]},\widehat{\alpha}_{\gamma}^{[t]})\text{ for all }t\geq 0.

(162)

Throughout, we will write
\langle\cdot\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}
for expectations only over
the Brownian motion
\mathbf{b}^{t}
, i.e. conditional
on
\mathbf{X},\bm{\theta}^{*},\bm{\varepsilon}
and the initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}
.

Lemma 5.6
.

Let us write the block forms

\mathbf{V}^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{U}^{t}&\ast\\
\mathbf{W}^{t}&\ast\end{pmatrix},\quad\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}&\ast\\
\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}&\ast\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{t})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{J}^{t}_{1}&\mathbf{J}^{t}_{2}\\
\mathbf{J}^{t}_{3}&\mathbf{J}^{t}_{4}\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathrm{d}u(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t})=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}&\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\\
\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}&\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\end{pmatrix}

with blocks of sizes
d
and
K
.
Fixing any
T>0
, on the event
\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\|\mathbf{y}\|\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d}\}
,
there is a constant
C>0
(depending on
T,C_{0}
but not on
\gamma
)
such that for any
\gamma>0
, we have

\begin{gathered}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C,\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{J}^{t}_{2}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d},\\
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{J}^{t}_{3}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C/\sqrt{d},\qquad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\lVert\mathbf{J}^{t}_{4}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C,\end{gathered}

(163)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\{\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\}\leq C,

(164)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\gamma,\quad\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\|_{F}\leq C\gamma/\sqrt{d}.

(165)

Furthermore, for some
\iota:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\iota(\gamma)=0
and for any initial condition

\mathbf{x}^{0}=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\frac{\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}}{\sqrt{d}},\frac{\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{2}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}}{\sqrt{d}},\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{3}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}},\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{4}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}\leq\iota(\gamma)\Big(\frac{\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert}{\sqrt{d}}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+1\Big).

(166)

Proof.

For (
163
), we have by definition that

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}

\displaystyle=-\beta\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}+\diag\Big[\Big(\partial_{\theta}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\Big)_{j=1}^{d}\Big],\quad\mathbf{J}_{2}^{t}=\Big(\nabla_{\alpha}s(\theta_{j}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})^{\top}\Big)_{j=1}^{d},

\displaystyle\mathbf{J}_{3}^{t}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\bm{\theta}}\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t},\mathsf{P}(\bm{\theta}^{t})),\quad\mathbf{J}_{4}^{t}=\mathrm{d}_{\alpha}\mathcal{G}(\widehat{\alpha}^{t},\mathsf{P}(\bm{\theta}^{t})),

and similarly for

\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}
.
Then the desired bounds (
163
) hold on the event where
\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0}
,
by Assumptions
2.2
and
2.3
for the derivatives
of
s(\cdot)
and
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
.

For (
164
), let us first prove the bounds for the discrete
dynamics
\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}\rVert_{F}
. By
definition, for each
t\in\gamma\mathbb{Z}_{+}
,

\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}=(\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t})\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}+\gamma\bar{\mathbf{J}}^{t}_{\gamma,2}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t},\quad\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+1}=\gamma\bar{\mathbf{J}}^{t}_{\gamma,3}\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}+(\mathbf{I}+\gamma\bar{\mathbf{J}}^{t}_{\gamma,4})\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}.

(167)

Then applying (
163
),

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq(1+C\gamma)\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+C\gamma\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F},\quad\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}\leq\frac{C\gamma}{\sqrt{d}}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+(1+C\gamma)\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F},

which further implies that

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}\leq(1+2C\gamma)\big(\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\big).

Iterating this bound from the initial conditions

\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{0}=\mathbf{I}_{d}
and
\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{0}=0_{K\times d}
shows
(
164
) for
\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}
and all
t\leq T
.
For the continuous version
\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
and
\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}\rVert_{F}
, note that analogously

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}^{t}=\mathbf{U}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathbf{J}^{s}_{1}\mathbf{U}^{s}+\mathbf{J}^{s}_{2}\mathbf{W}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,\quad\mathbf{W}^{t}=\mathbf{W}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathbf{J}^{s}_{3}\mathbf{U}^{s}+\mathbf{J}^{s}_{4}\mathbf{W}^{s})\mathrm{d}s

so
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}(\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}\rVert_{F})\leq C(\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}\rVert_{F})
. Then (
164
)
follows by Gronwall’s lemma.

For (
165
), we have by (
167
) and
(
163
)

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\Big(\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\Big)\leq C\gamma,

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\Big(\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\Big)\leq C\gamma/\sqrt{d}.

For (
166
), we have by the Lipschitz continuity of

s(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.2

that
\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}
is diagonal with

\lVert\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert)
. Next using the arguments that led to
(
109
), we have that on the event

\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\|\mathbf{y}\|\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d}\}
,
with
\mathbf{x}^{0}=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
,

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+\sqrt{d})

(168)

and

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}

\displaystyle\leq\iota(\gamma)(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{0}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\rVert+\sqrt{d}).

(169)

This implies the desired bound for
\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
, and a similar argument leads to the bound for

\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}_{2}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
. Next, by the
derivative bounds for
\mathcal{G}(\cdot)
in Assumption
2.3
, we have

\lVert\mathbf{J}^{3}_{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert/d+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert/\sqrt{d})
and
\lVert\mathbf{J}_{4}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C(\lVert\bm{\theta}^{t}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert/\sqrt{d}+\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{t}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert)
, hence the desired bounds also follow by (
169
).
∎

Lemma 5.7
.

Define

\mathcal{E}=\{\|\mathbf{X}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C_{0},\|\mathbf{y}\|\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d},\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|\leq C_{0}\sqrt{d},\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|\leq C_{0}\text{ for all
large }n,d\}.

(170)

Fixing any
T>0
, there exists a constant
C>0
(depending on
T,C_{0}
but not on

\gamma
) and a function
\iota:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying
\lim_{\gamma\to 0}\iota(\gamma)=0
, such that on
\mathcal{E}
, for any
\gamma>0

and all
0\leq s\leq t\leq T
,

\displaystyle|d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-d^{-1}\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])|

\displaystyle\leq\iota(\gamma)

(171)

\displaystyle|n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)-n^{-1}\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])|

\displaystyle\leq\iota(\gamma).

(172)

Proof.

Discretization of
\mathbf{R}_{\theta}
.

Let
\{P_{t}^{\gamma}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be the Markov semigroup for the discrete
dynamics (
119
),
i.e.
P_{t}^{\gamma}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}_{\gamma}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
. Then
applying Proposition
A.4
, for any
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

with
s<t
,

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\theta_{\gamma,j}^{t,(s,j),\varepsilon}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\gamma P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\partial_{j}P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x}).

This implies, for the given initial condition of the dynamics

\mathbf{x}^{0}=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
, that

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\partial_{j}P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x}^{0}).

Let
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
analogously denote the Markov semigroup of the
continuous dynamics (
4
–
5
), i.e.
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
. Then
applying Proposition
A.1
, for any
s,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
s\leq t
,

\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)=\sum_{j=1}^{d}P_{s}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}(\mathbf{x}^{0}).

Thus, for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}^{\gamma}_{\theta}([t]+1,[s])\Big|=\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{d}P_{s}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}(\mathbf{x}^{0})-\sum_{j=1}^{d}P_{[s]+1}^{\gamma}\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt\leq\underbrace{\Big|P_{s}\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}-\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}e_{j}\Big)(\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|}_{(I)}+\underbrace{\Big|(P_{s}-P_{[s]+1}^{\gamma})\Big(\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}e_{j}\Big)(\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|}_{(II)}.

Bound of
(I)
.
By Proposition

A.2
(c),
\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle(\mathbf{V}^{t-s})_{jj}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
, where
\{\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
are the solution to (
160
) with initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
.
Similarly, by Lemma
A.5
and the identification
(
162
),
\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\langle(\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor})_{jj}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
, where

\{\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
are the solution to
(
161
).
Let us write

\mathbf{V}^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{U}^{t}&\ast\\
\mathbf{W}^{t}&\ast\end{pmatrix},\quad\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}&\ast\\
\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}&\ast\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{t})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{J}_{1}^{t}&\mathbf{J}_{2}^{t}\\
\mathbf{J}_{3}^{t}&\mathbf{J}_{4}^{t}\end{pmatrix},\quad\mathrm{d}u(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t})=\begin{pmatrix}\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}&\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\\
\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}&\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\end{pmatrix}

with blocks of sizes
d
and
K
. Then

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}\mathbf{e}_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\langle\Tr\mathbf{U}^{t-s}-\Tr\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\Big|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t-s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t-s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+d\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t-s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}.

(173)

Since
|(t-s)-(\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor)|\leq C\gamma
,
the second term satisfies
\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}^{t-s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}^{t-s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C\gamma
by (
165
).
For the first term, note that by definition

\begin{gathered}\mathbf{U}^{t}=\mathbf{U}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathbf{J}^{s}_{1}\mathbf{U}^{s}+\mathbf{J}^{s}_{2}\mathbf{W}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,\quad\mathbf{W}^{t}=\mathbf{W}^{0}+\int_{0}^{t}(\mathbf{J}^{s}_{3}\mathbf{U}^{s}+\mathbf{J}^{s}_{4}\mathbf{W}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,\\
\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{U}^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}(\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{s}\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}+\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{s}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s})\mathrm{d}s,\quad\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}=\mathbf{W}^{0}+\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}(\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{s}\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}+\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{s}\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s})\mathrm{d}s.\end{gathered}

Hence

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big[\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{2}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\Big]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt+\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\Big[\langle\|\mathbf{J}^{s}_{1}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{U}^{s}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\|\mathbf{J}^{s}_{2}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{W}^{s}\|_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\Big]\mathrm{d}s

Let
C,C^{\prime}>0
be constants depending on
T
but not
\gamma
, and let

\iota(\gamma),\iota^{\prime}(\gamma)
be constants depending also on
\gamma

and satisfying
\iota(\gamma),\iota^{\prime}(\gamma)\to 0
as
\gamma\to 0
, all
changing from instance to instance.
By Lemma
5.6
, with
\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})
, we
have

\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C,\quad\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}\rVert_{F}\leq C,\quad\lVert\mathbf{J}_{1}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\leq C,\quad\lVert\mathbf{J}_{2}^{s}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{s}\rVert_{F}\leq C\sqrt{d},

\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{1}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}),\quad\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{2}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}),

hence

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\big(\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\big)\mathrm{d}s+\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}).

(174)

Next we have

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}\Big[\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{3}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\hskip 72.26999pt+\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{4}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{s}\rVert_{F}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\Big]\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 14.45377pt+\int_{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor}^{t}\Big[\langle\|\mathbf{J}^{s}_{3}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{U}^{s}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\langle\|\mathbf{J}^{s}_{4}\|_{F}\|\mathbf{W}^{s}\|_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\Big]\mathrm{d}s.

By Lemma
5.6
, we have also

\lVert\mathbf{J}_{3}^{s}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{s}\rVert_{F}\leq C/\sqrt{d},\quad\lVert\mathbf{J}_{4}^{s}\rVert_{F},\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{s}\rVert_{F}\leq C,

d\langle\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{3}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}),\quad\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{J}^{s}_{4}-\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{s}\rVert_{F}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}),

which implies that

\displaystyle\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}})\mathrm{d}s+\iota(\gamma)\Big(\frac{\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|}{\sqrt{d}}+\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|+1\Big).

(175)

Combining (
174
) and (
175
) yields

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}(\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{W}^{s}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}})\mathrm{d}s+\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d}),

so Gronwall’s lemma gives

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}+\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\mathbf{W}^{t}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+\sqrt{d})
.

Hence the bound (
173
) reads, for

\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})
,

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{t-s}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})-\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]-1}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})\Big|\leq\iota(\gamma)(\sqrt{d}\|\bm{\theta}\|+d\|\widehat{\alpha}\|+d).

(176)

Applying this with
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}^{s}=(\bm{\theta}^{s},\widehat{\alpha}^{s})
,
this implies that

\displaystyle(I)\leq\iota(\gamma)(\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+d\langle\lVert\widehat{\alpha}^{s}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+d)\leq\iota(\gamma)d,

the last step using the bound (
168
) and conditions
for
(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
on the event (
170
).

Bound of
(II)
.
Let
\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})=\sum_{j=1}^{d}\partial_{j}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}e_{j}(\mathbf{x})
. We first establish a Lipschitz bound for
\mathsf{f}
: Let
\{\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
and

\{\tilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be defined by
(
161
) with initializations
\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})

and
\tilde{\mathbf{x}}=(\tilde{\bm{\theta}},\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}})

respectively, coupled by the same Brownian motion. We write
\langle\cdot\rangle
for the average over this Brownian motion, and denote by
\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}
and

\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t},\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}

the blocks of
\tilde{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}
and
\mathrm{d}u(\tilde{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t})
.
Then, using
\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})=\langle\Tr\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}\rangle

with
\tau=\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor
as established above,

\displaystyle|\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})-\mathsf{f}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})|\leq|\langle\Tr\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}-\Tr\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}\rangle|\leq\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}\rVert_{F}\rangle.

(177)

We apply a similar argument as in term
(I)
, noting that

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+(1+\gamma\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}})\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}+\gamma\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F},

\displaystyle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\gamma\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}+\gamma\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\quad+\gamma\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}+(1+\gamma\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F})\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}.

By Lemma
5.6
, we have

\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C
,
and

\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}},\frac{\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}}{\sqrt{d}},\sqrt{d}\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F},\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\leq C
. Furthermore similar arguments to (
166
) in
Lemma
5.6
show that

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,1}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle,\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,2}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle,d\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,3}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle,\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{J}}_{\gamma,4}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\Big\langle\|\bar{\bm{\theta}}^{t}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{t}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}^{t}\|\Big\rangle\leq C^{\prime}\Big(\|\bm{\theta}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}\|\Big),

the quantities in the last expression denoting the differences in initial
conditions. Hence

\displaystyle\langle\lVert\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t+\gamma}\rVert_{F}\rangle

\displaystyle\leq(1+C\gamma)\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}+\sqrt{d}\lVert\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}-\tilde{\mathbf{W}}_{\gamma}^{t}\rVert_{F}\rangle+C\gamma\Big(\|\bm{\theta}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}\|\Big).

Iterating this bound gives

\langle\lVert\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}-\tilde{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}\rVert_{F}\rangle\leq C(\|\bm{\theta}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}\|)
,
which applied to (
177
) yields our desired Lipschitz bound

|\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})-\mathsf{f}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})|\leq C\sqrt{d}(\|\bm{\theta}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}\|).

Then, writing
\mathbf{x}^{0}=(\bm{\theta}^{0},\widehat{\alpha}^{0})
for the original initial
conditions,

\displaystyle(II)=\Big|(P_{s}-P_{[s]+1}^{\gamma})\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|=\Big|\langle\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x}^{s})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}-\langle\mathsf{f}(\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}\Big|\leq C\sqrt{d}\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}^{s}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\|\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}

where we couple
\{(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})\}_{t\geq 0}
and

\{\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
by
the same Brownian motion. Bounding

\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}\leq\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{s}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}+\langle\lVert\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\rVert\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}

and similarly for
\widehat{\alpha}
, and then applying
(
168
) and (
169
),
we obtain on the event (
170
) that

\langle\lVert\bm{\theta}^{s}-\bar{\bm{\theta}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\rVert+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}^{s}-\bar{\widehat{\alpha}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor+\gamma}\|\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}}\leq\iota(\gamma)(\|\bm{\theta}^{0}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}^{0}\|+\sqrt{d})\leq C\iota(\gamma)\sqrt{d}.

Hence also

(II)\leq\iota(\gamma)d.

The proof of (
171
) is completed
by combining the bounds for
(I)
and
(II)
.

Discretization of
\mathbf{R}_{\eta}
.

Let
P_{t}^{\gamma}
and
P_{t}
be the discrete and continuous Markov semigroups
defined above. Let
x_{i}(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\bm{\theta}
.
Introduce the matrix
\mathbf{E}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times(d+K)}
defined by

\mathbf{E}(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})=\bm{\theta}
, so that this reads
x_{i}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathbf{x}
for
\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})
.
Then for any
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}
with
s<t
, Proposition

A.4
gives

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\gamma P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\nabla P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}x_{i}(\mathbf{x}).

Let us introduce the shorthand
P_{t}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})=\langle\mathbf{x}_{\gamma}^{t}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

as a map
P_{t}^{\gamma}:\mathbb{R}^{d+K}\to\mathbb{R}^{d+K}
, so

P_{t}^{\gamma}x_{i}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}P_{t}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})
. Denote also

\mathrm{d}P_{t}^{\gamma}(\cdot):\mathbb{R}^{d+K}\to\mathbb{R}^{(d+K)\times(d+K)}
as the derivative of
this map
\mathbf{x}\mapsto P_{t}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})
. Then the above may be written as

\displaystyle\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}\langle\eta_{i}^{t,[s,i],\varepsilon}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\gamma P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\Big(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathrm{d}P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}(\cdot)^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}\Big)(\mathbf{x}),

implying that

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}(t,s)=\delta\beta^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{n}P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\Big(\mathbf{e}_{i}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\mathrm{d}P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}(\cdot)^{\top}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}\Big)(\mathbf{x}^{0})=\delta\beta^{2}P_{s+1}^{\gamma}\Tr\Big[\mathrm{d}P_{t-s-1}^{\gamma}(\cdot)\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\Big](\mathbf{x}^{0}).

By Proposition
A.1
, we have analogously
for any
s,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
s\leq t
that

\displaystyle\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)=\delta\beta^{2}P_{s}\Tr\big[\mathrm{d}P_{t-s}(\cdot)\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\big](\mathbf{x}^{0}).

Hence for all
s,t\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
with
s\leq t
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)-\gamma^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])\Big|

\displaystyle=\delta\beta^{2}\bigg[\underbrace{\Big|P_{s}\Tr\Big[\Big(\mathrm{d}P_{t-s}(\cdot)-\mathrm{d}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}(\cdot)\Big)\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\Big](\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|}_{(I)}+\underbrace{\Big|(P_{s}-P_{[s]+1}^{\gamma})\Tr\Big[\mathrm{d}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}(\cdot)\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\Big](\mathbf{x}^{0})\Big|}_{(II)}\bigg].

Bound of
(I)
.
Note that by Proposition

A.2
(c),
\Tr\mathrm{d}P_{t-s}(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=\langle\Tr\mathbf{V}^{t-s}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\langle\Tr\mathbf{U}^{t-s}\cdot\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
, where
\{\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
follow the
dynamics (
160
) and
\mathbf{U}^{t}
as before is the upper-left block of

\mathbf{V}^{t}
. Similarly, Lemma
A.5
yields that
\Tr\mathrm{d}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=\langle\Tr\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}=\langle\Tr\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}
, where
\{\bar{\mathbf{x}}_{\gamma}^{t},\bar{\mathbf{V}}_{\gamma}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
follow
(
161
). Hence, with
\mathbf{x}=(\bm{\theta},\widehat{\alpha})
,

\displaystyle\Big|\Tr\Big(\mathrm{d}P_{t-s}(\mathbf{x})-\mathrm{d}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})\Big)\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}\Big|

\displaystyle=\langle\Tr(\mathbf{U}^{t-s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor})\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\lVert\mathbf{X}\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^{2}\langle\lVert\mathbf{U}^{t-s}-\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor}\rVert_{F}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle\leq\iota(\gamma)(\sqrt{d}\lVert\bm{\theta}\rVert+d\lVert\widehat{\alpha}\rVert+d)

using the preceding bounds leading to (
176
). Then applying this with
\mathbf{x}=\mathbf{x}^{s}
shows
(I)\leq\iota(\gamma)d
.

Bound of
(II)
.
Let
\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})=\Tr\mathrm{d}P_{[t]-[s]}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{E}^{\top}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{E}=\Tr\langle\bar{\mathbf{U}}_{\gamma}^{\tau}\rangle_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}
, where
\tau=\left\lfloor t\right\rfloor-\left\lfloor s\right\rfloor
. By the
same arguments as above,

\displaystyle|\mathsf{f}(\mathbf{x})-\mathsf{f}(\tilde{\mathbf{x}})|\leq C\sqrt{d}(\|\bm{\theta}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|+\sqrt{d}\|\widehat{\alpha}-\tilde{\widehat{\alpha}}\|),

leading to
(II)\leq\iota(\gamma)d
. Combining these bounds for
(I)
and

(II)
shows (
172
).
∎

We now conclude the proof of Theorem
2.8
.

Proof of Theorem
2.8
.

The claims for
d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,s)
,
d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\theta}(t,*)
, and

n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{C}_{\eta}(t,s)
follow immediately from the definitions of these
quantities, Corollary
2.6
applied with
f_{\theta}(\theta^{s},\theta^{t})=\theta^{s}\theta^{t}
,
f_{\theta}(\theta^{*},\theta^{t})=\theta^{*}\theta^{t}
,
f_{\eta}(\eta^{*},\varepsilon,\eta^{s},\eta^{t})=\delta\beta^{2}(\eta^{s}-\eta^{*}-\varepsilon)(\eta^{t}-\eta^{*}-\varepsilon)
,
and an application of the dominated convergence theorem to take expectations over
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\in[0,T]}
in the almost-sure convergence statements of
Corollary
2.6
.

For the claim for
d^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)
, for any
s,t\in[0,T]
with

s\leq t
, by Lemma
5.7
, almost surely

\displaystyle\limsup_{n,d\to\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-\frac{1}{\gamma}\cdot\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])\Big|\leq\iota(\gamma).

By Lemma
5.1

and the identification (
99
) of
Lemma
4.3
, almost surely

\displaystyle\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{\gamma}\cdot\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])=\frac{1}{\gamma}\,R_{\theta}^{\gamma}([t]+1,[s])=\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+\gamma,s).

The bound (
104
) implies uniform convergence of

\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
to
R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
as
\gamma\to 0
,
and
R_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t,s)
is continuous in

s,t
by Theorem
2.4
and the definition of the space

\mathcal{S}^{\text{cont}}
. Thus

\lim_{\gamma\to 0}|\bar{R}_{\theta}^{\gamma}(t+\gamma,s)-R_{\theta}(t,s)|=0.

Then, taking the limit
n,d\to\infty
followed by
\gamma\to 0
shows almost surely

\lim_{n,d\to\infty}\Big|\frac{1}{d}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\theta}(t,s)-R_{\theta}(t,s)\Big|=0.

The proof of the claim for
n^{-1}\Tr\mathbf{R}_{\eta}(t,s)
is the same.
∎

Appendix A
Existence of linear response functions

A.1
Continuous dynamics

Fix any dimension
m\geq 1
, and consider the function classes

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}

\displaystyle=\Big\{f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}\text{ twice continuously-differentiable}:\,\nabla f(\mathbf{x}),\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x})\text{ are globally bounded}\Big\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}

\displaystyle=\Big\{f:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}\text{ twice continuously-differentiable}:

\displaystyle\hskip 36.135pt\nabla f_{i}(\mathbf{x}),\nabla^{2}f_{i}(\mathbf{x})\text{ are globally bounded
and H\"{o}lder-continuous for each }i=1,\ldots,m\Big\}.

We consider a general stochastic diffusion over
\mathbf{x}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
given by

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t}=u(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t}

(178)

where
\mathbf{b}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
is a standard Brownian motion,
u(\cdot)
a Lipschitz
drift function, and
\mathbf{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
a deterministic diffusion
coefficient matrix. We note that
the joint evolution of
\mathbf{x}^{t}=(\bm{\theta}^{t},\widehat{\alpha}^{t})
in
(
4
–
5
) is of this form, with
m=d+K
and with
u(\cdot)
and
\mathbf{M}
as defined in
(
159
).
The conditions of Theorem
2.8
ensure that this drift
function
u(\cdot)
satisfies
u\in\mathcal{B}
.

We prove in this section the following result:

Proposition A.1
.

Suppose
u\in\mathcal{B}
, and let
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solution of
(
178
) with initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
.
For any
a\in\mathcal{A}
,
b\in\mathcal{B}
, and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, define

R(t,s)=P_{s}\big(b^{\top}\nabla P_{t-s}a\big)(\mathbf{x})

(179)

where
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]
. Then
\{R(t,s)\}_{0\leq s\leq t}

is the unique continuous function for which the following holds:

Let
h:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
be any continuous bounded
function, and for each
\varepsilon>0
let
\{\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solution
of the perturbed dynamics

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}=\Big(u(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})+\varepsilon h(t)b(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\Big)\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t}

(180)

with the same initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}
. Then for any
t>0
,

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}]-\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]\Big)=\int_{0}^{t}R(t,s)h(s)\mathrm{d}s.

Statements similar to Proposition
A.1
have been
established in
[
55
,
56
]
. Our setting here
is somewhat non-standard, in that
\mathbf{M}
may be rank-degenerate, so
the PDE describing the law of
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is not uniformly elliptic.
We show Proposition
A.1
in two steps, first deriving
regularity estimates for the Markov semigroup
\{P_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}

in such settings using the results of
[
63
]
, and then
applying the proof idea of

[
56
, Theorem 3.9]
with these regularity estimates in
place of the Schauder estimates derived therein from uniform ellipticity.

We will write

P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}}=\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]

(181)

for the Markov semigroup associated to (
178
). When the initial
condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
is clear from context, we will abbreviate

\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}}
.
We denote the infinitesimal generator
\mathrm{L}
of this semigroup by

\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x})=u(\mathbf{x})^{\top}\nabla f(\mathbf{x})+\Tr\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}).

(182)

Throughout this section, constants
C,C^{\prime},c>0
may depend on the dimension
m

and the functions
u,a,b
.

Proposition A.2
.

Suppose the assumptions of Proposition
A.1
hold.
Let
u_{i}:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}
be the
i^{\text{th}}
coordinate of
u
,
and let
\partial_{j}u_{i}
and
\partial_{j}\partial_{k}u_{i}

be its first-order and second-order partial derivatives.

(a)

For each
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, the diffusion (
178
) has a
unique solution
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
with initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
.
Furthermore, there exists a modification
\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
of this solution for each
initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
such that
\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
is jointly continuous in

(t,\mathbf{x})
and twice continuously-differentiable in
\mathbf{x}
.

(b)

For every
i=1,\ldots,m
, let
x_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
be the
i^{\text{th}}

coordinate of
\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
, and let
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})=\nabla x_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{m}

and
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})=\nabla^{2}x_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
be its gradient and
Hessian in
\mathbf{x}
.
Then
(\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))
are solutions to the first and second
variation processes

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}u_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}=\big(\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}u_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\mathbf{v}_{k}^{t\top}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}u_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{H}_{j}^{t}\big)\mathrm{d}t\end{cases}

(183)

with initial conditions
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{0}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{e}_{i}
(the
i^{\text{th}}
standard basis vector
in
\mathbb{R}^{m}
) and
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{0}(\mathbf{x})=0
.

Furthermore

\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})\|_{2},\|\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{Ct}
for some
C>0
and
all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
t\geq 0
.

(c)

For any
f\in\mathcal{A}
, the map
(t,\mathbf{x})\mapsto P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
is
continuously-differentiable in
t
and twice continuously-differentiable in

\mathbf{x}
, and furthermore
\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x}),\nabla^{2}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
are uniformly
bounded over
t\in[0,T]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
for any fixed
T>0
.
For any
t\geq 0
and initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
,
letting
(\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t},\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t})\equiv(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))

be as defined in parts (a) and (b), we have

\displaystyle\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\bigg\rangle

(184)

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\bigg\langle\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\mathbf{v}_{k}^{t\top}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{H}_{j}^{t}\bigg\rangle

and

\partial_{t}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=P_{t}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{L}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x}).

(185)

Proof.

Since the coordinates of
u\in\mathcal{B}
are Lipschitz with bounded and
Hölder-continuous first and second derivatives, part (a) follows directly from

[
63
, Theorems II.1.2, II.3.3]
.

For part (b), since
u\in\mathcal{B}
has bounded and Hölder-continuous first
derivative,
[
63
, Theorem II.3.1]
shows that

\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
has derivative
\mathbf{V}^{t}(\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{d}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}^{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}

solving the first-variation equation

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{V}^{t}=[\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{t})]\mathbf{V}^{t}\mathrm{d}t,\qquad\mathbf{V}^{0}=\mathbf{I}.

Noting that
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}=\nabla x_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})

is (the transpose of) the
i^{\text{th}}
row of
\mathbf{V}^{t}
, this gives the first
equation of (
183
) with initial condition
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{0}=\mathbf{e}_{i}
.
Next, consider the joint diffusion

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t})=P(\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t})\mathrm{d}t+\sqrt{2}(\mathbf{M}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{t},0),\qquad P(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{V})=\Big(u(\mathbf{x}),[\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x})]\mathbf{V}\Big).

The condition
u\in\mathcal{B}
implies also that

P(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{V})
has bounded and Hölder-continuous first derivative

\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{V})
, which we identify as a square matrix of dimension
(m+m^{2})\times(m+m^{2})

under the vectorization of
\mathbf{V}
. Then

[
63
, Theorem II.3.1]
applied again
shows that
(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}),\mathbf{V}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))
has
derivative
\mathbf{U}^{t}=\mathrm{d}_{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{V})}(\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t})\in\mathbb{R}^{(m+m^{2})\times(m+m^{2})}

solving the second-variation equation

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{U}^{t}=[\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{x}^{t},\mathbf{V}^{t})]\mathbf{U}^{t}\mathrm{d}t,\qquad\mathbf{U}^{0}=\mathbf{I}.

(186)

Noting that
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}=\nabla^{2}x_{i}^{t}(\mathbf{x})

is the block of
\mathbf{U}^{t}
corresponding to
\mathrm{d}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}
,
and that the block corresponding to
\mathrm{d}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{x}^{t}
is
\mathbf{V}^{t}
,
one may check that the restriction of (
186
)
to the
\mathrm{d}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}
block gives exactly the second equation of
(
183
) with initialization
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{0}=0
.
If
C>0
is an upper bound for
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}}\|\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathrm{op}}

and
\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}}\|\mathrm{d}P(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{V})\|_{\mathrm{op}}
, then integrating these equations
gives
\|\mathbf{V}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{Ct}\|\mathbf{V}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=e^{Ct}

and
\|\mathbf{U}^{t}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{Ct}\|\mathbf{U}^{0}\|_{\mathrm{op}}=e^{Ct}
, which implies
the bounds for
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}
and
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}
.

For part (c), consider any
f\in\mathcal{A}
. Applying (b) and the chain rule,

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}

(187)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\mathbf{v}_{k}^{t\top}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{H}_{j}^{t}.

By parts (a–b) and the condition
f\in\mathcal{A}
, for any
T>0
,
the right sides of (
187
) are uniformly bounded and
continuous in
(t,\mathbf{x})
over
t\in[0,T]
. Then dominated convergence
implies that
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
is twice continuously-differentiable in
\mathbf{x}
, that

\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}}=\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\rangle

and
\nabla^{2}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}}=\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\rangle
, and that
these are also uniformly bounded and continuous over
t\in[0,T]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
.

For the derivative in
t
, by Itô’s formula

f(\mathbf{x}^{t})=f(\mathbf{x})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{s})\mathrm{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}^{s})^{\top}\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{s}

where
\mathrm{L}
is the generator defined in (
182
).
Since
\nabla f(\mathbf{x}^{s})
is bounded over
s\in[0,t]
and
\mathbf{x}^{s}
is adapted to
the filtration of
\{\mathbf{b}^{s}\}
, the last term is a
martingale, so taking expectations gives

P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=f(\mathbf{x})+\int_{0}^{t}\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{s})\rangle\mathrm{d}s.

Hence, differentiating in
t
, for any
t>0
we have

\partial_{t}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=P_{t}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}).

(188)

By Jensen’s inequality, for any
s,t\geq 0
, we have

\langle(P_{s}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}))^{2}\rangle\leq\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t+s})^{2}\rangle=\langle(u(\mathbf{x}^{t+s})^{\top}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}^{t+s})+\Tr\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}^{t+s}))^{2}\rangle\leq C(1+\langle\|\mathbf{x}^{t+s}\|_{2}^{2}\rangle),

the last
inequality holding for some
C>0
by boundedness of
\nabla f,\nabla^{2}f

and the Lipschitz continuity of
u
. Then

[
63
, Theorem II.2.1]
implies that

P_{s}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))
is uniformly bounded in
L^{2}
over compact domains of
s,t\geq 0
and of the initial condition
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
,
and hence is also uniformly integrable over these domains.
This uniform integrability for
s=0
and
dominated convergence shows that

\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle
in (
188
)
is continuous in
(t,\mathbf{x})
, and hence
P_{t}f
is continuously-differentiable in

t
. Taking the limit
t\to 0
in (
188
), also

\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x})=\lim_{t\to 0}\partial_{t}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
. Then
applying this with
P_{t}f\in\mathcal{A}
in place of
f
,

\mathrm{L}P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\lim_{s\to 0}\partial_{s}P_{t+s}f(\mathbf{x})=\lim_{s\to 0}\partial_{s}\langle P_{s}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle\overset{(*)}{=}\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=P_{t}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}).

Here, to justify
(*)
, we note that
\partial_{s}P_{s}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})=P_{s}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})

by (
188
), so
(*)
follows from uniform integrability of
this quantity and dominated convergence to take the limit

\lim_{s\to 0}\partial_{s}\langle P_{s}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=\lim_{s\to 0}\langle P_{s}\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle=\langle\mathrm{L}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle
.
Combining with (
188
), this shows all claims about

\partial_{t}P_{t}f
in part (c).
∎

Now consider the perturbed dynamics (
180
) for any

\varepsilon>0
. Let us denote the perturbed drift as

u^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})=u(\mathbf{x})+\varepsilon h(t)b(\mathbf{x}).

For any
t\geq s\geq 0
, we define its (time inhomogeneous)
Markov semigroup and infinitesimal generator

P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}=\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}],\qquad\mathrm{L}_{t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=u^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x})^{\top}\nabla f(\mathbf{x})+\Tr\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\nabla^{2}f(\mathbf{x}).

The following extends the semigroup regularity estimates of
Proposition
A.2
to this perturbed process.

Proposition A.3
.

Suppose the assumptions of Proposition
A.1
hold.
Then for any
f\in\mathcal{A}
, the map
(s,t,\mathbf{x})\mapsto P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})

is continuously-differentiable in
(s,t)
and twice continuously-differentiable
in
\mathbf{x}
, and furthermore
\nabla P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}),\nabla^{2}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})

are uniformly bounded over
s,t\in[0,T]
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
for any fixed
T>0
. We have

\partial_{t}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}\mathrm{L}_{t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}),\qquad\partial_{s}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})={-}\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}).

(189)

Proof.

We omit the superscript
\varepsilon
and write
\mathbf{x}^{t}\equiv\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}
.
The same arguments as in Proposition
A.2
using

[
63
, Theorems II.1.2, II.3.1, II.3.3]
show,
for each
s\geq 0
and
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, there exists a modification
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})\}_{t\geq s}
of the
solution to (
180
) with initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}
,
such that
\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})
is jointly continuous in
(s,t,\mathbf{x})
and twice
continuously-differentiable in
\mathbf{x}
. Each component

x_{i}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})
of this solution has gradient
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}=\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}x_{i}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})

and Hessian
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}=\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}^{2}x_{i}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})
solving

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}u_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}=\big(\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\partial_{j}\partial_{k}u_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}{\mathbf{v}_{k}^{t}}^{\top}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}u_{i}^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{H}_{j}^{t}\big)\mathrm{d}t\end{cases}

with initial conditions
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{s}(s,\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{e}_{i}
and
\mathbf{H}_{i}^{s}(s,\mathbf{x})=0
. Furthermore,

\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})\|_{2},\|\mathbf{H}_{i}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq e^{C(t-s)}
for some
C>0

and all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
and
t\geq s\geq 0
.

Then for any
f\in\mathcal{A}
, the same dominated convergence argument
as in Proposition
A.2

shows that
P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
is twice continuously-differentiable in
\mathbf{x}
,
where its first and second derivatives are uniformly bounded and continuous in

(s,t,\mathbf{x})
over
s,t\in[0,T]
and may be computed by
differentiating in
\mathbf{x}
under the integral. The same argument as in
Proposition
A.2
using Itô’s formula shows also that

P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
is continuously-differentiable in
t
, with

\partial_{t}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}\mathrm{L}_{t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle\mathrm{L}_{t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}.

For the derivative in
s
, we have by Itô’s formula for any
h>0
,

P_{s-h,s}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{s})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s-h}=\mathbf{x}}=f(\mathbf{x})+\int_{s-h}^{s}\langle\mathrm{L}_{r}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}^{r})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s-h}=\mathbf{x}}\,\mathrm{d}r.

The same argument as in Proposition
A.2
shows that

\mathrm{L}_{t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(s,\mathbf{x}))
is uniformly integrable over compact domains of
t\geq s\geq 0
and of
\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}
, so by dominated convergence
we have
\lim_{h\downarrow 0,\,\,r\uparrow s}\langle\mathrm{L}_{r}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}^{r})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s-h}=\mathbf{x}}=\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}(\mathbf{x})
.
So taking the limit
h\to 0
above and rearranging shows

\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=\lim_{h\downarrow 0}\frac{P_{s-h,s}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})-f(\mathbf{x})}{h}.

(190)

Then for any
s\leq t
, applying this to
P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f\in\mathcal{A}

in place of
f
gives

\lim_{h\downarrow 0}\frac{P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})-P_{s-h,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})}{h}=\lim_{h\downarrow 0}\frac{P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})-P_{s-h,s}^{\varepsilon}(P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f)(\mathbf{x})}{h}={-}\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x}),

i.e.
P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
is left-differentiable in
s
. Here

{-}\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})={-}u^{\varepsilon}(s,\mathbf{x})^{\top}\nabla P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})-\Tr\mathbf{M}\mathbf{M}^{\top}\nabla^{2}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
is continuous in
(s,t,\mathbf{x})

by the continuity of
\nabla P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f
and

\nabla^{2}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f
argued above. Then

P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
is also continuously-differentiable in
s
with

\partial_{s}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})={-}\mathrm{L}_{s}^{\varepsilon}P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
A.1
.

Let
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and

\{\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the solutions to the
unperturbed and perturbed diffusions. Let
\{P_{t}\}
and
\mathrm{L}
be the semigroup
and infinitesimal generator for
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
, and let

\{P^{\varepsilon}_{s,t}\}
and
\mathrm{L}^{\varepsilon}_{t}
be those for
\{\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}\}_{t\geq 0}
.
We write
\partial_{s},\partial_{t}
for the derivatives in
s,t
and reserve

\nabla f(t,\mathbf{x})
for the gradient of
f
in its second argument
\mathbf{x}
.

For any
t>s
and
r\in[s,t]
, define

f^{\varepsilon}(r,\mathbf{x})=P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})
. Then by Itô’s formula applied to
the unperturbed process
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
,

f^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}^{t})=f^{\varepsilon}(s,\mathbf{x}^{s})+\int_{s}^{t}(\partial_{r}+\mathrm{L})f^{\varepsilon}(r,\mathbf{x}^{r})\mathrm{d}r+\int_{s}^{t}\nabla f^{\varepsilon}(r,\mathbf{x}^{r})^{\top}\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}\mathrm{d}\mathbf{b}^{r}.

Proposition
A.3
shows

P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a\in\mathcal{A}
, so
\nabla f^{\varepsilon}(r,\mathbf{x}^{r})
is uniformly bounded and the last term is a
martingale. Then, taking expectations under the initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}

and applying (
189
),

\displaystyle\langle a(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}=\langle f^{\varepsilon}(t,\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}

\displaystyle=f^{\varepsilon}(s,\mathbf{x})+\int_{s}^{t}\langle(\partial_{r}+\mathrm{L})f^{\varepsilon}(r,\mathbf{x}^{r})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})+\int_{s}^{t}\langle({-}\mathrm{L}_{r}^{\varepsilon}+\mathrm{L})P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x}^{r})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})-\int_{s}^{t}\varepsilon h(r)\langle(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x}^{r})\rangle_{\mathbf{x}^{s}=\mathbf{x}}\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})-\varepsilon\int_{s}^{t}h(r)P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}r.

Applying this also with
\varepsilon=0
and
P_{s,t}^{0}=P_{t-s}
and
taking the difference, we obtain the identity

P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})-P_{t-s}a(\mathbf{x})=\varepsilon\int_{s}^{t}h(r)P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}r.

(191)

From the definition of
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
and
form of
\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})
in (
184
), we have

\displaystyle P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Big\langle(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x}^{r-s})\Big\rangle_{\mathbf{x}_{0}=\mathbf{x}},

(192)

\displaystyle\nabla P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=\Bigg\langle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\partial_{x_{i}}[b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a](\mathbf{x}^{r-s})\mathbf{v}_{i}^{r-s}\Bigg\rangle_{\mathbf{x}_{0}=\mathbf{x}}.

(193)

Since
b\in\mathcal{B}
is Lipschitz by assumption, and

P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a\in\mathcal{A}
by Proposition
A.3
, we have

|(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x}^{r-s})|,\,|\partial_{x_{i}}[b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a](\mathbf{x}^{r-s})|\leq C(1+\|\mathbf{x}^{r-s}\|_{2})

for some
C>0
. Then these quantities are uniformly integrable over bounded
domains of
s\leq r\leq t
and
\mathbf{x}
,
by
[
63
, Theorem II.2.1]
.
Furthermore
\|\mathbf{v}_{i}^{r-s}\|_{2}
is bounded by
Proposition
A.2
(b), so the integrands on the right sides
of both (
192
–
193
) are also uniformly integrable over these
domains. Then applying dominated convergence, we may
differentiate (
191
) in
\mathbf{x}
under the integral to
obtain

\nabla P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})-\nabla P_{t-s}a(\mathbf{x})=\varepsilon\int_{s}^{t}h(r)\nabla P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{r,t}^{\varepsilon}a)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}r,

and take the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
to get

\nabla P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})\to\nabla P_{t-s}a(\mathbf{x})
. Applying this with
s=r

to the right side of (
191
), and
taking the limit
\varepsilon\to 0
in (
191
) using uniform
integrability of (
192
), we arrive at

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{P_{s,t}^{\varepsilon}a(\mathbf{x})-P_{t-s}a(\mathbf{x})}{\varepsilon}=\int_{s}^{t}h(r)P_{r-s}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{t-r}a)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}r.

For
s=0
, this means

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\langle a(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\rangle-\langle a(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle\Big)=\int_{0}^{t}h(r)P_{r}(b^{\top}\nabla P_{t-r}a)(\mathbf{x})\mathrm{d}r,

verifying that (
21
)
holds with response function
R(t,s)
given by (
179
).
Continuity of this function
R(t,s)
in
(s,t)
follows from the above uniform
integrability statements, together with continuity of

t\mapsto\nabla P_{t}(\mathbf{x})
in
t
as shown in Proposition

A.2
.

For uniqueness, observe that if
\tilde{R}(t,s)
is any continuous function
different from
R(t,s)
, then they must differ on a subset of
(s,t)
of
positive Lebesgue measure. Then there exists a continuous
bounded function
h:[0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}
such that
\int_{0}^{t}R(t,s)h(s)\mathrm{d}s\neq\int_{0}^{t}\tilde{R}(t,s)h(s)\mathrm{d}s
, implying that

\tilde{R}
cannot satisfy (
21
).
Thus this response function
R(t,s)
is unique.
∎

A.2
Discrete dynamics

We record (elementary) analogues of the preceding results for discrete dynamics

\mathbf{x}^{t+1}=\mathbf{x}^{t}+u(\mathbf{x}^{t})+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})

(194)

where
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is a Gaussian process with
\mathbf{b}^{0}=0

and independent increments
\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\gamma\mathbf{I})
, for some

\gamma>0
. The following is an analogue of Proposition

A.1
.

Proposition A.4
.

Suppose
u:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
is Lipschitz, and let
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}

be the solution of (
194
) with initial condition

\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
. For any Lipschitz functions
a:\mathbb{R}^{m}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
and
b:\mathbb{R}^{m}\to\mathbb{R}^{m}
, define

R(t,s)=P_{s}\big(b^{\top}P(\nabla P_{t-s-1}a)\big)(\mathbf{x})

where
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]
. Then for any
s,t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}

with
s<t
,

R(t,s)=\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}]-\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]\Big)

where
\{\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
is the solution of the perturbed dynamics

\mathbf{x}^{t+1,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon}+u(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})+\varepsilon b(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\mathbf{1}_{s=t}+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t})

with the same initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}
.

Proof.

Write as shorthand
P=P_{1}
. If
f
is
L
-Lipschitz, then
(coupling the processes with initializations
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
by the same
\{\mathbf{b}^{t}\}
)

\displaystyle|Pf(\mathbf{x})-Pf(\mathbf{y})|
