\displaystyle=\Big|\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}+u(\mathbf{x})-\sqrt{2}\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{b}^{1})]-\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{y}+u(\mathbf{y})-\sqrt{2}\mathbf{M}\,\mathbf{b}^{1})]\Big|\leq L(1+L_{u})\|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}\|

where
L_{u}
is the Lipschitz constant of
u
. Hence
Pf
is Lipschitz, so
P_{t}f

is Lipschitz for all
t\geq 0
.

Let
P_{t}^{\varepsilon}
be the Markov semigroup for the dynamics

\mathbf{x}^{t+1}=\mathbf{x}^{t}+u(\mathbf{x}^{t})+\varepsilon b(\mathbf{x}^{t})+\sqrt{2}\,\mathbf{M}(\mathbf{b}^{t+1}-\mathbf{b}^{t}),

and write as shorthand
P^{\varepsilon}=P_{1}^{\varepsilon}
. Then by definition,

\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}]=P_{s}P^{\varepsilon}P_{t-s-1}(\mathbf{x}),

so

\lim_{\varepsilon\to 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\Big(\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t,\varepsilon})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0,\varepsilon}=\mathbf{x}]-\mathbb{E}[a(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]\Big)=\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}P_{s}P^{\varepsilon}P_{t-s-1}a(\mathbf{x}).

(195)

Note that for any
L
-Lipschitz function
f
, we have

\partial_{\varepsilon}P^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=\partial_{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}+u(\mathbf{x})+\varepsilon b(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{b}^{1})]=b(\mathbf{x})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\nabla f(\mathbf{x}+u(\mathbf{x})+\varepsilon b(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{b}^{1})]

(196)

where the derivative may be taken under the expectation by dominated
convergence. In particular,

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}P^{\varepsilon}f(\mathbf{x})=b(\mathbf{x})^{\top}\mathbb{E}[\nabla f(\mathbf{x}+u(\mathbf{x})+\mathbf{b}^{1})]=b(\mathbf{x})^{\top}P(\nabla f)(\mathbf{x}).

The derivative (
196
) is also
bounded for all
\varepsilon\geq 0
by
L\|b(\mathbf{x})\|
, which is
integrable under
P_{s}
since
b
is Lipschitz.
Then again by dominated convergence,

\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}P_{s}P^{\varepsilon}P_{t-s-1}a(\mathbf{x})=P_{s}\partial_{\varepsilon}|_{\varepsilon=0}P^{\varepsilon}P_{t-s-1}a(\mathbf{x})=P_{s}(b^{\top}P(\nabla P_{t-s-1}a))(\mathbf{x}),

and the result follows from applying this to (
195
).
∎

The following is an analogue of the first statement of (
184
).

Lemma A.5
.

Let
\{\mathbf{x}^{t}\}_{t\in\mathbb{Z}_{+}}
be the solution to (
194
)
where
u(\cdot)
is Lipschitz, and consider the first variation processes

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t+1}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{v}_{i}^{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}u_{i}(\mathbf{x}^{t})\cdot\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}

with initializations
\mathbf{v}_{i}^{0}=\mathbf{e}_{i}
. Denote
P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mid\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}]=\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\rangle
.
Then for any Lipschitz function
f:\mathbb{R}^{d+K}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\bigg\langle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}\bigg\rangle.

Proof.

Stacking
\mathbf{V}^{t}=[\mathbf{v}_{1}^{t},\ldots,\mathbf{v}_{m}^{t}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
with initial
condition
\mathbf{V}^{0}=\mathbf{I}_{m}
, the evolution of
\mathbf{V}^{t}
is

\mathbf{V}^{t+1}=[\mathbf{I}+\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{t})]\mathbf{V}^{t}

where
\mathrm{d}u
is the derivative of
u(\cdot)
. Writing
\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
for the
dependence of
\mathbf{x}^{t}
on the initial condition
\mathbf{x}^{0}=\mathbf{x}
, and writing

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
for its derivative in
\mathbf{x}
, by the chain rule we
have
\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t+1}(\mathbf{x})=[\mathbf{I}+\mathrm{d}u(\mathbf{x}^{t})]\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
, with initial condition

\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{0}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{I}
. Thus
(\mathbf{V}^{t})^{\top}=\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})
for all
t\geq 0
, so

\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))=[\mathrm{d}\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x})]^{\top}\nabla f(\mathbf{x}^{t})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial_{j}f(\mathbf{x}^{t})\mathbf{v}_{j}^{t}.

By dominated convergence we have
\nabla P_{t}f(\mathbf{x})=\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}\langle f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\rangle=\langle\nabla_{\mathbf{x}}f(\mathbf{x}^{t}(\mathbf{x}))\rangle
,
and the result follows.
∎
